## Preface / 序言
===Original===

This is an account of a zoo-geographic reconnaissance through the Brazilian hinterland.
The official and proper title of the expedition is that given it by the Brazilian Government: Expedição Scientifica Roosevelt-Rondon. When I started from the United States, it was to make an expedition, primarily concerned with mammalogy and ornithology, for the American Museum of Natural History of New York. This was undertaken under the auspices of Messrs. Osborn and Chapman, acting on behalf of the Museum. In the body of this work I describe how the scope of the expedition was enlarged, and how it was given a geographic as well as a zoological character, in consequence of the kind proposal of the Brazilian Secretary of State for Foreign Affairs, General Lauro Müller. In its altered and enlarged form the expedition was rendered possible only by the generous assistance of the Brazilian Government. Throughout the body of the work will be found reference after reference to my colleagues and companions of the expedition, whose services to science I have endeavored to set forth, and for whom I shall always feel the most cordial friendship and regard.
Theodore Roosevelt.
Sagamore Hill, September 1, 1914

===Chinese===

本书记述的是一次穿越巴西腹地的动物地理勘察（zoo-geographic reconnaissance）。
本次考察队的正式名称由巴西政府所定：罗斯福-龙东科学考察队（Expedição Scientifica Roosevelt-Rondon）。我从美国启程时，原计划是为纽约美国自然历史博物馆（American Museum of Natural History）进行一次以哺乳动物学（mammalogy）和鸟类学（ornithology）为主要任务的考察。此行承蒙奥斯本先生与查普曼先生代表博物馆鼎力主持。在本书正文中，我记述了考察范围如何扩大，又如何因巴西外交部长劳罗·米勒将军（General Lauro Müller）的善意提议，从单纯的动物学考察扩展为兼具地理学性质的考察。考察队经此扩充改组之后，唯有依靠巴西政府的慷慨协助方能成行。正文各处屡屡提及考察队的诸位同僚与旅伴，我已尽力阐扬他们为科学所做的贡献，而对他们，我将永怀最诚挚的友情与敬意。
西奥多·罗斯福
萨加莫尔山（Sagamore Hill），1914年9月1日

## I The Start / 第一章 启程
===Original===

One day in 1908, when my presidential term was coming to a close, Father Zahm, a priest whom I knew, came in to call on me. Father Zahm and I had been cronies for some time, because we were both of us fond of Dante and of history and of science﻿—I had always commended to theologians his book, *Evolution and Dogma*. He was an Ohio boy, and his early schooling had been obtained in old-time American fashion in a little log school; where, by the way, one of the other boys was Januarius Aloysius MacGahan, afterward the famous war correspondent and friend of Skobeloff. Father Zahm told me that MacGahan even at that time added an utter fearlessness to chivalric tenderness for the weak, and was the defender of any small boy who was oppressed by a larger one. Later Father Zahm was at Notre Dame University, in Indiana, with Maurice Egan, whom, when I was President, I appointed minister to Denmark.
On the occasion in question Father Zahm had just returned from a trip across the Andes and down the Amazon, and came in to propose that after I left the presidency he and I should go up the Paraguay into the interior of South America. At the time I wished to go to Africa, and so the subject was dropped; but from time to time afterward we talked it over. Five years later, in the spring of 1913, I accepted invitations conveyed through the governments of Argentina and Brazil to address certain learned bodies in these countries. Then it occurred to me that, instead of making the conventional tourist trip purely by sea round South America, after I had finished my lectures I would come north through the middle of the continent into the valley of the Amazon; and I decided to write Father Zahm and tell him my intentions. Before doing so, however, I desired to see the authorities of the American Museum of Natural History, in New York City, to find out whether they cared to have me take a couple of naturalists with me into Brazil and make a collecting trip for the museum.
Accordingly, I wrote to Frank Chapman, the curator of ornithology of the museum, and accepted his invitation to lunch at the museum one day early in June. At the lunch, in addition to various naturalists, to my astonishment I also found Father Zahm; and as soon as I saw him I told him I was now intending to make the South American trip.

===Chinese===

1908年的一天，我的总统任期即将届满，一位我相识的神父——扎姆神父（Father Zahm）——前来拜访。扎姆神父与我相交已久，因为我们都钟爱但丁、历史与科学——我一直向神学家们推荐他的著作《进化与教义》（Evolution and Dogma）。他是俄亥俄州人，早年按老式美国做派在一所小小的原木校舍里接受启蒙教育；顺带一提，当时同学中有一位贾纳里乌斯·阿洛伊修斯·麦加汉（Januarius Aloysius MacGahan），日后成了著名的战地记者、斯科别列夫的朋友。扎姆神父告诉我，麦加汉那时便既有对弱者的侠骨柔肠，又浑身是胆，凡有小孩子受大孩子欺负，他必挺身保护。后来扎姆神父与莫里斯·伊根（Maurice Egan）同在印第安纳州的圣母大学（Notre Dame University）求学；我任总统时曾任命伊根为驻丹麦公使。
那次来访时，扎姆神父刚作了一次翻越安第斯山脉（the Andes）、顺亚马逊河（the Amazon）而下的旅行，前来提议：待我卸任总统之后，由他和我一同沿巴拉圭河上溯，深入南美洲腹地。当时我一心想去非洲，此事便搁下了；但此后我们不时还会谈起。五年后，即1913年春，我接受经阿根廷和巴西两国政府转达的邀请，前往两国向若干学术团体发表演讲。这时我想到，与其循惯例纯由海路环南美作一趟游客式旅行，不如在讲学结束后取道大陆中部北上，进入亚马逊河流域；于是我决定写信告诉扎姆神父我的打算。不过在此之前，我想先去见见纽约市美国自然历史博物馆（American Museum of Natural History）的负责人，问问他们是否有意让我带几位博物学家同赴巴西，为博物馆作一次采集之旅。
于是我致信该馆鸟类学主任弗兰克·查普曼，并应他之邀于六月初的某日在馆中午餐。席间除诸位博物学家外，竟还有扎姆神父在座，令我大为惊讶；我一见到他便告诉他，我如今是决意要作这趟南美之行了。

===Original===

It appeared that he had made up his mind that he would take it himself, and had actually come on to see Mr. Chapman to find out if the latter could recommend a naturalist to go with him; and he at once said he would accompany me. Chapman was pleased when he found out that we intended to go up the Paraguay and across into the valley of the Amazon, because much of the ground over which we were to pass had not been covered by collectors. He saw Henry Fairfield Osborn, the president of the museum, who wrote me that the museum would be pleased to send under me a couple of naturalists, whom, with my approval, Chapman would choose.
The men whom Chapman recommended were Messrs. George K. Cherrie and Leo E. Miller. I gladly accepted both. The former was to attend chiefly to the ornithology and the latter to the mammalogy of the expedition; but each was to help out the other. No two better men for such a trip could have been found. Both were veterans of the tropical American forests. Miller was a young man, born in Indiana, an enthusiastic naturalist with good literary as well as scientific training. He was at the time in the Guiana forests, and joined us at Barbados. Cherrie was an older man, born in Iowa, but now a farmer in Vermont. He had a wife and six children. Mrs. Cherrie had accompanied him during two or three years of their early married life in his collecting trips along the Orinoco. Their second child was born when they were in camp a couple of hundred miles from any white man or woman. One night a few weeks later they were obliged to leave a camping-place, where they had intended to spend the night, because the baby was fretful, and its cries attracted a jaguar, which prowled nearer and nearer in the twilight until they thought it safest once more to put out into the open river and seek a new resting-place. Cherrie had spent about twenty-two years collecting in the American tropics. Like most of the field-naturalists I have met, he was an unusually efficient and fearless man; and willy-nilly he had been forced at times to vary his career by taking part in insurrections. Twice he had been behind the bars in consequence, on one occasion spending three months in a prison of a certain South American state, expecting each day to be taken out and shot.

===Chinese===

原来他已拿定主意自己也要作此行，并且确实已来见查普曼先生，问他能否推荐一位博物学家随行；他当即表示愿与我同行。查普曼得知我们打算沿巴拉圭河上溯、穿越大陆进入亚马逊河流域后很是欣喜，因为我们将途经的地方，有很大一部分还从未有采集者踏足。他去见了馆长亨利·费尔菲尔德·奥斯本（Henry Fairfield Osborn）；奥斯本致函于我，说博物馆乐于派两名博物学家随行，人选由查普曼物色，经我认可即可。
查普曼推荐的是乔治·K·切里（George K. Cherrie）和利奥·E·米勒（Leo E. Miller）两位先生，我欣然接受了两人。前者主要负责考察队的鸟类学（ornithology）工作，后者主要负责哺乳动物学（mammalogy）工作，但两人须互相协助。这样一趟旅行，再也找不到比他们俩更合适的人了。两人都是久经美洲热带丛林锤炼的老手。米勒是位年轻人，生于印第安纳州，是一位热忱的博物学家，既受过良好的科学训练，也有很好的文学修养。他当时正在圭亚那丛林中，到巴巴多斯（Barbados）才与我们会合。切里年长些，生于艾奥瓦州，如今是佛蒙特州的农场主，有妻子和六个孩子。结婚头两三年里，切里太太曾随他一同在奥里诺科河（the Orinoco）沿岸作采集旅行。他们的第二个孩子出生时，两人正在营地里，方圆数百英里内再无一个白人。几周后的一个夜晚，婴儿啼哭不止，哭声引来了一头美洲豹（jaguar），在暮色中越踅越近，他们只得离开原打算过夜的那处营地——他们觉得还是再划到开阔的河面上、另觅歇息之处最为稳妥。
切里在美洲热带地区从事采集已约二十二年。与我见过的多数野外博物学家一样，他干练过人、胆气十足；而且身不由己，有时还不得不参与叛乱，给自己的职业生涯添些变数。他为此两度锒铛入狱，其中一次在某个南美国家的监狱里蹲了三个月，天天都以为要被拉出去枪毙。

===Original===

In another state he had, as an interlude to his ornithological pursuits, followed the career of a gunrunner, acting as such off and on for two and a half years. The particular revolutionary chief whose fortunes he was following finally came into power, and Cherrie immortalized his name by naming a new species of ant-thrush after him﻿—a delightful touch, in its practical combination of those not normally kindred pursuits, ornithology and gunrunning.
In Anthony Fiala, a former arctic explorer, we found an excellent man for assembling equipment and taking charge of its handling and shipment. In addition to his four years in the arctic regions, Fiala had served in the New York Squadron in Puerto Rico during the Spanish War, and through his service in the squadron had been brought into contact with his little Tennessee wife. She came down with her four children to say goodbye to him when the steamer left. My secretary, Mr. Frank Harper, went with us. Jacob Sigg, who had served three years in the United States Army, and was both a hospital nurse and a cook, as well as having a natural taste for adventure, went as the personal attendant of Father Zahm. In southern Brazil my son Kermit joined me. He had been bridge building, and a couple of months previously, while on top of a long steel span, something went wrong with the derrick, he and the steel span coming down together on the rocky bed beneath. He escaped with two broken ribs, two teeth knocked out, and a knee partially dislocated, but was practically all right again when he started with us.
In its composition ours was a typical American expedition. Kermit and I were of the old Revolutionary stock, and in our veins ran about every strain of blood that there was on this side of the water during colonial times. Cherrie’s father was born in Ireland, and his mother in Scotland; they came here when very young, and his father served throughout the Civil War in an Iowa cavalry regiment. His wife was of old Revolutionary stock. Father Zahm’s father was an Alsacian immigrant, and his mother was partly of Irish and partly of old American stock, a descendant of a niece of General Braddock. Miller’s father came from Germany, and his mother from France.

===Chinese===

在另一个国家，他曾在从事鸟类学研究之余兼做军火走私，断断续续干了两年半。他追随的那位革命首领最终上了台，切里用他的名字命名了一种新的蚁鸫（ant-thrush），使此人名垂不朽——把鸟类学与军火走私这两门素不相干的行当如此务实地合于一处，堪称妙笔。
至于装备的筹集、搬运与发运，我们找到了一位极合适的人选——曾赴北极探险的安东尼·菲亚拉（Anthony Fiala）。除在北极地区待过四年外，菲亚拉还在美西战争期间于波多黎各的纽约骑兵队服役，并因这段军旅结识了他那位田纳西姑娘——他后来的妻子。轮船启航时，她带着四个孩子来为他送行。我的秘书弗兰克·哈珀（Frank Harper）先生与我们同行。雅各布·西格（Jacob Sigg）在美国陆军服役过三年，既当过医院护工又会做饭，又天性喜爱冒险，此行充任扎姆神父的贴身随从。在巴西南部，我的儿子克米特与我会合。他此前在造桥，两个月前，他在一段长钢梁顶上作业时，起重机出了故障，人和钢梁一起摔到了下面的岩石河床上。他落得两根肋骨骨折、两颗牙磕掉、膝盖半脱臼，但到与我们一同启程时，已基本痊愈。
就人员构成而言，我们这支考察队是典型的美国式队伍。我和克米特出身于独立战争时代的老家族，殖民时期大洋此岸的几乎每一种血统，都在我们血管里流淌。切里的父亲生于爱尔兰，母亲生于苏格兰；两人年纪很小时便来到美国，父亲在整个南北战争期间在艾奥瓦州的一支骑兵部队服役。他的妻子也出身独立战争老家族。扎姆神父的父亲是阿尔萨斯移民，母亲则一半是爱尔兰血统，一半是老美国家世，是布雷多克将军一位侄女的后代。米勒的父亲来自德国，母亲来自法国。

===Original===

Fiala’s father and mother were both from Bohemia, being Czechs, and his father had served four years in the Civil War in the Union Army﻿—his Tennessee wife was of old Revolutionary stock. Harper was born in England, and Sigg in Switzerland. We were as varied in religious creed as in ethnic origin. Father Zahm and Miller were Catholics, Kermit and Harper Episcopalians, Cherrie a Presbyterian, Fiala a Baptist, Sigg a Lutheran, while I belonged to the Dutch Reformed Church.
For arms the naturalists took 16-bore shotguns, one of Cherrie’s having a rifle barrel underneath. The firearms for the rest of the party were supplied by Kermit and myself, including my Springfield rifle, Kermit’s two Winchesters, a .405 and .30-40, the Fox 12-gauge shotgun, and another 16-gauge gun, and a couple of revolvers, a Colt and a Smith & Wesson. We took from New York a couple of canvas canoes, tents, mosquito-bars, plenty of cheesecloth, including nets for the hats, and both light cots and hammocks. We took ropes and pulleys which proved invaluable on our canoe trip. Each equipped himself with the clothing he fancied. Mine consisted of khaki, such as I wore in Africa, with a couple of United States Army flannel shirts and a couple of silk shirts, one pair of hobnailed shoes with leggings, and one pair of laced leather boots coming nearly to the knee. Both the naturalists told me that it was well to have either the boots or leggings as a protection against snakebites, and I also had gauntlets because of the mosquitoes and sandflies. We intended where possible to live on what we could get from time to time in the country, but we took some United States Army emergency rations, and also ninety cans, each containing a day’s provisions for five men, made up by Fiala.
The trip I proposed to take can be understood only if there is a slight knowledge of South American topography. The great mountain chain of the Andes extends down the entire length of the western coast, so close to the Pacific Ocean that no rivers of any importance enter it. The rivers of South America drain into the Atlantic. Southernmost South America, including over half of the territory of the Argentine Republic, consists chiefly of a cool, open plains country.

===Chinese===

菲亚拉的父母都是波希米亚来的捷克人，父亲曾在南北战争中于北军服役四年——他那位田纳西州妻子则是独立战争老世家出身。哈珀生于英格兰，西格生于瑞士。我们的宗教信仰同血统出身一样五花八门：扎姆神父和米勒是天主教徒，克米特和哈珀信圣公会，切里是长老会信徒，菲亚拉是浸信会信徒，西格信路德宗，而我属荷兰归正会。
武器方面，两位博物学家带的是16口径猎枪，其中切里的一支枪管下方还加了一根步枪枪管。其余人员的枪械由克米特和我提供，包括我的斯普林菲尔德步枪、克米特的两支温彻斯特步枪（一支.405口径、一支.30-40口径）、福克斯12口径霰弹枪、另一支16口径枪，以及两支左轮手枪——一支柯尔特、一支史密斯-韦森。我们从纽约带了两条帆布独木舟、帐篷、蚊帐，还有大量粗纱布（cheesecloth）——包括帽子上用的纱网——以及轻便行军床和吊床。我们还带了绳索和滑轮，在后来的独木舟之行中证明是无价之宝。各人自行置办合意的衣着。我的行头是卡其布服装——与我在非洲穿的一样——外加两件美国陆军法兰绒衬衫、两件丝绸衬衫、一双带绑腿的钉底鞋，以及一双几乎及膝的系带皮靴。两位博物学家都告诉我，穿高帮靴或绑腿有助于防蛇咬；因为蚊蚋和沙蝇之故，我还备了长筒手套。我们打算在可能的条件下就地取食，但仍带了些美国陆军应急口粮，另有九十听罐头，每听为五人一日的给养，皆由菲亚拉配好。
我拟作的这趟旅行，须对南美地形稍有了解方能明白。安第斯山脉这条巨大的山链纵贯整个西海岸，紧逼太平洋，以至没有任何重要河流注入该洋。南美洲的河流都注入大西洋。南美洲最南端，包括阿根廷共和国一半以上的领土，主要是凉爽的开阔平原。

===Original===

Northward of this country, and eastward of the Andes, lies the great bulk of the South American continent, which is included in the tropical and the subtropical regions. Most of this territory is Brazilian. Aside from certain relatively small stretches drained by coast rivers, this immense region of tropical and subtropical America east of the Andes is drained by the three great river systems of the Plate, the Amazon, and the Orinoco. At their headwaters the Amazon and the Orinoco systems are actually connected by a sluggish natural canal. The headwaters of the northern affluents of the Paraguay and the southern affluents of the Amazon are sundered by a stretch of high land, which toward the east broadens out into the central plateau of Brazil. Geologically this is a very ancient region, having appeared above the waters before the dawning of the age of reptiles, or, indeed, of any true land vertebrates on the globe. This plateau is a region partly of healthy, rather dry and sandy, open prairie, partly of forest. The great and low-lying basin of the Paraguay, which borders it on the south, is one of the largest, and the still greater basin of the Amazon, which borders it on the north, is the very largest of all the river basins of the earth.
In these basins, but especially in the basin of the Amazon, and thence in most places northward to the Caribbean Sea, lie the most extensive stretches of tropical forest to be found anywhere. The forests of tropical West Africa, and of portions of the Farther-Indian region, are the only ones that can be compared with them. Much difficulty has been experienced in exploring these forests, because under the torrential rains and steaming heat the rank growth of vegetation becomes almost impenetrable, and the streams difficult of navigation; while white men suffer much from the terrible insect scourges and the deadly diseases which modern science has discovered to be due very largely to insect bites. The fauna and flora, however, are of great interest. The American Museum was particularly anxious to obtain collections from the divide between the headwaters of the Paraguay and the Amazon, and from the southern affluents of the Amazon.

===Chinese===

由此往北，在安第斯山脉以东，便是南美大陆的主体，属于热带和亚热带区域。这片领土大部分属于巴西。除若干由沿海短河切割的较小地带外，安第斯山脉以东这片热带与亚热带美洲的广袤地域，都由拉普拉塔河、亚马逊河（the Amazon）和奥里诺科河（the Orinoco）三大水系排泄水量。在源头处，亚马逊河水系与奥里诺科河水系实际上由一条水流滞缓的天然水道相连。巴拉圭河北侧支流与亚马逊河南侧支流的源头之间，隔着一片高地；这片高地向东延展，豁然开阔为巴西中部高原。从地质上说，这是一片极为古老的地区，早在爬行动物时代来临之前——实际上早在地球上任何真正的陆生脊椎动物出现之前——便已出水成陆。这片高原，一部分是宜人的、相当干燥多沙的开阔草原，一部分是森林。南面与之接壤的巴拉圭河巨大低洼盆地是世界上最大的流域之一，而北面与之接壤的亚马逊河流域更为宏大，是地球上所有河流盆地中最大的一个。
在这些盆地中——尤其是在亚马逊盆地，由此向北直至加勒比海的大部分地方——分布着举世无双的大片热带森林。只有西非热带森林和远印地区部分地带的森林差可与之比拟。勘察这些森林历尽艰辛，因为在大雨滂沱、暑气蒸腾之下，草木疯长，几乎无路可通，河流亦难航行；白人更苦于可怕的虫害，以及那些现代科学已查明大半由虫叮引发的致命疾病。然而那里的动物群与植物群极具研究价值。美国自然历史博物馆极想获得巴拉圭河与亚马逊河源头分水岭一带以及亚马逊河南侧各支流地区的标本收藏。

===Original===

Our purpose was to ascend the Paraguay as nearly as possible to the head of navigation, thence cross to the sources of one of the affluents of the Amazon, and if possible descend it in canoes built on the spot. The Paraguay is regularly navigated as high as boats can go. The starting-point for our trip was to be Asuncion, in the state of Paraguay.
My exact plan of operations was necessarily a little indefinite, but on reaching Rio de Janeiro the minister of foreign affairs, Mr. Lauro Müller, who had been kind enough to take great personal interest in my trip, informed me that he had arranged that on the headwaters of the Paraguay, at the town of Cáceres, I would be met by a Brazilian Army colonel, himself chiefly Indian by blood, Colonel Rondon. Colonel Rondon has been for a quarter of a century the foremost explorer of the Brazilian hinterland. He was at the time in Manaos, but his lieutenants were in Cáceres and had been notified that we were coming.
More important still, Mr. Lauro Müller﻿—who is not only an efficient public servant but a man of wide cultivation, with a quality about him that reminded me of John Hay﻿—offered to help me make my trip of much more consequence than I had originally intended. He has taken a keen interest in the exploration and development of the interior of Brazil, and he believed that my expedition could be used as a means toward spreading abroad a more general knowledge of the country. He told me that he would cooperate with me in every way if I cared to undertake the leadership of a serious expedition into the unexplored portion of western Matto Grosso, and to attempt the descent of a river which flowed nobody knew whither, but which the best-informed men believed would prove to be a very big river, utterly unknown to geographers. I eagerly and gladly accepted, for I felt that with such help the trip could be made of much scientific value, and that a substantial addition could be made to the geographical knowledge of one of the least-known parts of South America. Accordingly, it was arranged that Colonel Rondon and some assistants and scientists should meet me at or below Corumbá, and that we should attempt the descent of the river, of which they had already come across the headwaters.
I had to travel through Brazil, Uruguay, the Argentine, and Chile for six weeks to fulfil my speaking engagements.

===Chinese===

我们的目的是沿巴拉圭河尽可能上溯至航路尽头，再越过分水岭抵达亚马逊河某条支流的源头，如有可能，便乘在当地打造的独木舟顺流而下。巴拉圭河上，船只能开到哪里，定期航班就通到哪里。我们此行的起点定在巴拉圭国的亚松森（Asuncion）。
我的具体行动计划难免有些含糊，但一到里约热内卢（Rio de Janeiro），外交部长劳罗·米勒先生——承他美意，对我的旅行极为关切——便告诉我，他已安排妥当：在巴拉圭河上游的卡塞雷斯（Cáceres）城，将有一位巴西陆军上校前来与我会合；此人血统以印第安人为主，便是隆东上校（Colonel Rondon）。二十五年来，隆东上校一直是巴西腹地首屈一指的勘察者。他当时人在马瑙斯（Manaos），但他的几位中尉都在卡塞雷斯，已接到通知说我们将至。
更要紧的是，米勒先生——他不但是干练的公仆，还是一位学识广博之士，身上有种气质令我想起约翰·海——主动提出帮我此行变得远比原计划更为重要。他对勘察和开发巴西内陆素来热心，认为我们这支考察队可借以让世人更广泛地了解巴西。他对我说，倘若我愿意率一支正经的考察队深入马托格罗索（Matto Grosso）西部未勘之地，并尝试顺一条河漂流而下——此河流向何方无人知晓，但消息最灵通的人士相信它必是一条大河，地理学家对之一无所知——他愿全力与我合作。我热切而欣然地接受了，因为我感到，有这样的帮助，此行便可具有重大科学价值，还能为南美洲这片最不为人知的地区之一充实可观的地理知识。于是安排停当：隆东上校偕若干助手和科学家在科伦巴（Corumbá）或其下游与我会合，然后我们尝试沿河下漂——那条河的源头，他们此前已经寻获。
为了履行演讲之约，我还须在巴西、乌拉圭、阿根廷和智利旅行六个星期。

===Original===

Fiala, Cherrie, Miller, and Sigg left me at Rio, continuing to Buenos Aires in the boat in which we had all come down from New York. From Buenos Aires they went up the Paraguay to Corumbá, where they awaited me. The two naturalists went first, to do all the collecting that was possible; Fiala and Sigg travelled more leisurely, with the heavy baggage.
---
Before I followed them I witnessed an incident worthy of note from the standpoint of a naturalist, and of possible importance to us because of the trip we were about to take. South America, even more than Australia and Africa, and almost as much as India, is a country of poisonous snakes. As in India, although not to the same degree, these snakes are responsible for a very serious mortality among human beings. One of the most interesting evidences of the modern advance in Brazil is the establishment near São Paulo of an institution especially for the study of these poisonous snakes, so as to secure antidotes to the poison and to develop enemies to the snakes themselves. We wished to take into the interior with us some bottles of the anti-venom serum, for on such an expedition there is always a certain danger from snakes. On one of his trips Cherrie had lost a native follower by snakebite. The man was bitten while out alone in the forest, and, although he reached camp, the poison was already working in him, so that he could give no intelligible account of what had occurred, and he died in a short time.
Poisonous snakes are of several different families, but the most poisonous ones, those which are dangerous to man, belong to the two great families of the colubrine snakes and the vipers. Most of the colubrine snakes are entirely harmless, and are the common snakes that we meet everywhere. But some of them, the cobras for instance, develop into what are on the whole perhaps the most formidable of all snakes. The only poisonous colubrine snakes in the New World are the ring-snakes, the coral-snakes of the genus Elaps, which are found from the extreme southern United States southward to the Argentine. These coral-snakes are not vicious and have small teeth which cannot penetrate even ordinary clothing. They are only dangerous if actually trodden on by someone with bare feet or if seized in the hand.

===Chinese===

菲亚拉、切里、米勒和西格在里约与我分手，乘我们大家从纽约乘来的那条船继续前往布宜诺斯艾利斯（Buenos Aires），再从那里溯巴拉圭河而上到科伦巴，在那里等我。两位博物学家先行，沿途尽量采集；菲亚拉和西格带着沉重行李，走得从容些。
---
在我赶去与他们会合之前，亲眼见识了一件事，从博物学家的角度看值得一记，又因我们即将开始的旅行而于我们或有切身的意义。南美洲是个毒蛇之国，比澳大利亚和非洲尤甚，几乎与印度不相上下。与印度一样（虽程度稍逊），这些蛇造成了人类非常严重的死亡。巴西近代进步最有趣的例证之一，便是在圣保罗（São Paulo）近旁设立了一所专门研究这些毒蛇的机构，以求获得蛇毒的解药，并培殖毒蛇的天敌。我们想带几瓶抗蛇毒血清（anti-venom serum）进内陆，因为这样的考察队总有几分遭蛇之险。切里有一回旅行，一名土著随从便死于蛇咬：那人独自在林中时被咬，虽挣扎回到营地，蛇毒已然发作，说不清究竟出了什么事，不多时便死了。
毒蛇分属几个不同的科，但毒性最强、对人有害的那些，都归于游蛇（colubrine snakes）和蝰蛇两大类。游蛇大多完全无害，是我们随处可见的普通蛇类；但其中一些，例如眼镜蛇（cobra），发展成为总体而言也许是所有蛇类中最可怕的毒蛇。新大陆唯一有毒的游蛇是环蛇，即眼镜蛇属（Elaps）的珊瑚蛇，分布自美国极南端向南直至阿根廷。这些珊瑚蛇性情并不凶恶，牙齿细小，连普通衣物都咬不穿，只有被人赤脚踩到或被人徒手抓住时才有危险。

===Original===

There are harmless snakes very like them in color which are sometimes kept as pets; but it behooves every man who keeps such a pet or who handles such a snake to be very sure as to the genus to which it belongs.
The great bulk of the poisonous snakes of America, including all the really dangerous ones, belong to a division of the widely spread family of vipers which is known as the pit-vipers. In South America these include two distinct subfamilies or genera﻿—whether they are called families, subfamilies, or genera would depend, I suppose, largely upon the varying personal views of the individual describer on the subject of herpetological nomenclature. One genus includes the rattlesnakes, of which the big Brazilian species is as dangerous as those of the southern United States. But the large majority of the species and individuals of dangerous snakes in tropical America are included in the genus *Lachecis*. These are active, vicious, aggressive snakes without rattles. They are exceedingly poisonous. Some of them grow to a very large size, being indeed among the largest poisonous snakes in the world﻿—their only rivals in this respect being the diamond rattlesnake of Florida, one of the African mambas, and the Indian hamadryad, or snake-eating cobra. The fer-de-lance, so dreaded in Martinique, and the equally dangerous bushmaster of Guiana are included in this genus. A dozen species are known in Brazil, the biggest one being identical with the Guiana bushmaster, and the most common one, the jararaca, being identical, or practically identical with the fer-de-lance. The snakes of this genus, like the rattlesnakes and the Old World vipers and puff-adders, possess long poison-fangs which strike through clothes or any other human garment except stout leather. Moreover, they are very aggressive, more so than any other snakes in the world, except possibly some of the cobras. As, in addition, they are numerous, they are a source of really frightful danger to scantily clad men who work in the fields and forests, or who for any reason are abroad at night.
The poison of venomous serpents is not in the least uniform in its quality.

===Chinese===

有几种颜色与之酷似的无害蛇，有时被人当宠物养；但凡养这种宠物或摆弄这种蛇的人，务必弄清它所属的属。
美洲毒蛇的绝大多数，包括所有真正危险的种类，都属于广布的蝰蛇科中称为蝮蛇（坑蝰，pit-vipers）的一支。在南美洲，这一支包括两个不同的亚科或属——究竟该称科、亚科还是属，我想主要取决于各描述者对爬行动物命名问题的个人见解。一属包括各种响尾蛇（rattlesnake），其中巴西的大型种类与美国南部的响尾蛇同样危险。但热带美洲危险蛇类的大多数种和个体都属于矛头蝮属（Lachecis）。这是一些无响环、行动敏捷、性情凶狠、富于攻击性的蛇，毒性极强。其中一些长得非常大，确实位居世界最大毒蛇之列——在这一方面堪与匹敌的，只有佛罗里达的菱斑响尾蛇、非洲的某种曼巴蛇，以及印度的眼镜王蛇（即食蛇的眼镜蛇）。马提尼克岛闻之色变的枪头蛇（fer-de-lance）和圭亚那同样危险的布什马斯特蛇（bushmaster）都属此属。巴西已知的此类蛇约有十二种，最大的一种与圭亚那布什马斯特蛇同种，最常见的雅拉拉卡蛇（jararaca）则与枪头蛇同种，或几乎同种。此属的蛇与响尾蛇及旧大陆的蝰蛇、鼓腹毒蛇一样，长有长毒牙，隔着衣物或任何别的衣料都能咬中，唯独厚皮革除外。而且，它们非常好斗，除某些眼镜蛇外，比世界上任何其他蛇都好斗。加之数量众多，对于衣着单薄、在田间林中劳作的人，或因故夜间在外的人，实在是一大可怕的危险。
毒蛇的蛇毒，其性质绝非千篇一律。

===Original===

On the contrary, the natural forces﻿—to use a term which is vague, but which is as exact as our present-day knowledge permits﻿—that have developed in so many different families of snakes these poisoned fangs have worked in two or three totally different fashions. Unlike the vipers, the colubrine poisonous snakes have small fangs, and their poison, though on the whole even more deadly, has entirely different effects, and owes its deadliness to entirely different qualities. Even within the same family there are wide differences. In the jararaca an extraordinary quantity of yellow venom is spurted from the long poison-fangs. This poison is secreted in large glands which, among vipers, give the head its peculiar ace-of-spades shape. The rattlesnake yields a much smaller quantity of white venom, but, quantity for quantity, this white venom is more deadly. It is the great quantity of venom injected by the long fangs of the jararaca, the bushmaster, and their fellows that renders their bite so generally fatal. Moreover, even between these two allied genera of pit-vipers, the differences in the action of the poison are sufficiently marked to be easily recognizable, and to render the most effective anti-venomous serum for each slightly different from the other. However, they are near enough alike to make this difference, in practice, of comparatively small consequence. In practice the same serum can be used to neutralize the effect of either, and, as will be seen later on, the snake that is immune to one kind of venom is also immune to the other.
But the effect of the venom of the poisonous colubrine snakes is totally different from, although to the full as deadly as, the effect of the poison of the rattlesnake or jararaca. The serum that is an antidote as regards the colubrines. The animal that is immune to the bite of one may not be immune to the bite of the other. The bite of a cobra or other colubrine poisonous snake is more painful in its immediate effects than is the bite of one of the big vipers. The victim suffers more. There is a greater effect on the nerve-centres, but less swelling of the wound itself, and, whereas the blood of the rattlesnake’s victim coagulates, the blood of the victim of an elapine snake﻿—that is, of one of the only poisonous American colubrines﻿—becomes watery and incapable of coagulation.

===Chinese===

恰恰相反，在如此众多不同的蛇科中孕育出这些毒牙的自然力量——此词虽嫌含混，但已是当今知识所许可的最确切说法——是以两三种截然不同的方式发挥作用的。与蝰蛇不同，有毒的游蛇毒牙短小；它们的蛇毒虽总体上更加致命，效用却完全不同，其致命性源于全然不同的特性。甚至同一科内也千差万别：雅拉拉卡蛇能从长毒牙中喷出数量惊人的黄色毒液，毒液由大腺体分泌，正是这些腺体使蝰蛇头部呈独特的黑桃A形；响尾蛇的白色毒液产量少得多，但同等剂量下，这种白毒更为致命。正是雅拉拉卡蛇、布什马斯特蛇及其同类以长牙注入的大量毒液，使它们的咬伤往往致命。此外，即便在这两个近缘的蝮蛇属之间，蛇毒作用之差也明显到一眼可辨，以致各自最有效的抗蛇毒血清也略有不同。不过两者毕竟相近，这一差别在实际上无甚大碍。实际上，同一种血清便可中和两者的毒性，而且如后文所见，对一种蛇毒免疫的蛇，对另一种也免疫。
但有毒游蛇的蛇毒，其效力与响尾蛇或雅拉拉卡蛇的蛇毒虽同样致命，性质却截然不同。对游蛇之毒具有解毒作用的血清，则全然是另一回事。对一种咬伤免疫的动物，对另一种的咬伤未必免疫。眼镜蛇或其他有毒游蛇咬伤的即时反应，比大蝰蛇的咬伤更疼痛，受害者更痛苦：对神经中枢的影响更大，但伤口本身肿胀较轻；而且，响尾蛇受害者的血液会凝固，而眼镜蛇亚科蛇类——即美洲唯一有毒的游蛇——受害者的血液则变得稀薄如水，无法凝固。

===Original===

Snakes are highly specialized in every way, including their prey. Some live exclusively on warm-blooded animals, on mammals, or birds. Some live exclusively on batrachians, others only on lizards, a few only on insects. A very few species live exclusively on other snakes. These include one very formidable venomous snake, the Indian hamadryad, or giant cobra, and several nonpoisonous snakes. In Africa I killed a small cobra which contained within it a snake but a few inches shorter than itself; but, as far as I could find out, snakes were not the habitual diet of the African cobras.
The poisonous snakes use their venom to kill their victims, and also to kill any possible foe which they think menaces them. Some of them are good-tempered, and only fight if injured or seriously alarmed. Others are excessively irritable, and on rare occasions will even attack of their own accord when entirely unprovoked and unthreatened.
On reaching São Paulo on our southward journey from Rio to Montevideo, we drove out to the “Instituto Serumthérapico,” designed for the study of the effects of the venom of poisonous Brazilian snakes. Its director is Doctor Vital Brazil, who has performed a most extraordinary work and whose experiments and investigations are not only of the utmost value to Brazil but will ultimately be recognized as of the utmost value for humanity at large. I know of no institution of similar kind anywhere. It has a fine modern building, with all the best appliances, in which experiments are carried on with all kinds of serpents, living and dead, with the object of discovering all the properties of their several kinds of venom, and of developing various anti-venom serums which nullify the effects of the different venoms. Every effort is made to teach the people at large by practical demonstration in the open field the lessons thus learned in the laboratory. One notable result has been the diminution in the mortality from snakebites in the province of São Paulo.
In connection with his institute, and right by the laboratory, the doctor has a large serpentarium, in which quantities of the common poisonous and nonpoisonous snakes are kept, and some of the rarer ones.

===Chinese===

蛇类在方方面面都高度特化，包括猎物在内。有些专吃温血动物，即哺乳动物或鸟类；有些专吃两栖类，有些只吃蜥蜴，少数只吃昆虫。极少数几种专吃别的蛇，其中包括一种非常可怕的有毒蛇——印度的眼镜王蛇（hamadryad，即巨眼镜蛇），以及若干无毒蛇。在非洲我打死过一条小眼镜蛇，腹中有一条仅比它短几英寸的蛇；但据我所能查明的，蛇并非非洲眼镜蛇的惯常食物。
毒蛇用毒液杀死猎物，也用它杀死自认为威胁到它们的任何天敌。有些脾气好，只有受了伤或被严重惊扰才出手；另一些则极其易怒，偶尔甚至在毫无挑衅、毫无威胁的情况下主动出击。
在从里约南下蒙得维的亚（Montevideo）途中经过圣保罗时，我们驱车前往“血清疗法研究所”（Instituto Serumthérapico），该所专为研究巴西毒蛇蛇毒的效用而设。所长是维塔尔·布拉西尔医生（Doctor Vital Brazil），他完成了极为不凡的工作，其实验与探究不仅对巴西具有最高价值，最终也将被公认为对全人类具有最高价值。据我所知，别处没有任何同类机构。它拥有一座漂亮的现代化楼宇，配备各种最佳设备，在里面以种种活蛇死蛇作实验，以求查明各类蛇毒的全部特性，并研制能消解各种蛇毒效用的多种抗蛇毒血清（anti-venom serum）。还千方百计通过在野外的实地演示，把实验室里学得的知识传授给普通民众。一个显著成果是圣保罗省蛇咬致死人数的下降。
与研究所相连、紧挨实验室，医生还建了一座大型养蛇场，里面养着大量常见的有毒与无毒蛇，也有若干珍稀种类。

===Original===

He has devoted considerable time to the effort to find out if there are any natural enemies of the poisonous snakes of his country, and he has discovered that the most formidable enemy of the many dangerous Brazilian snakes is a nonpoisonous, entirely harmless, rather uncommon Brazilian snake, the mussurama. Of all the interesting things the doctor showed us, by far the most interesting was the opportunity of witnessing for ourselves the action of the mussurama toward a dangerous snake.
The doctor first showed us specimens of the various important snakes, poisonous and nonpoisonous, in alcohol. Then he showed us preparations of the different kinds of venom and of the different anti-venom serums, presenting us with some of the latter for our use on the journey. He has been able to produce two distinct kinds of anti-venom serum, one to neutralize the virulent poison of the rattlesnake’s bite, the other to neutralize the poison of the different snakes of the *Lachecis* genus. These poisons are somewhat different and moreover there appear to be some differences between the poisons of the different species of *Lachecis*; in some cases the poison is nearly colorless, and in others, as in that of the jararaca, whose poison I saw, it is yellow.
But the vital difference is that between all these poisons of the pit-vipers and the poisons of the colubrine snakes, such as the cobra and the coral-snake. As yet the doctor has not been able to develop an anti-venom serum which will neutralize the poison of these colubrine snakes. Practically this is a matter of little consequence in Brazil, for the Brazilian coral-snakes are dangerous only when mishandled by someone whose bare skin is exposed to the bite. The numerous accidents and fatalities continually occurring in Brazil are almost always to be laid to the account of the several species of *Lachecis* and the single species of rattlesnake.
Finally, the doctor took us into his lecture-room to show us how he conducted his experiments. The various snakes were in boxes, on one side of the room, under the care of a skilful and impassive assistant, who handled them with the cool and fearless caution of the doctor himself. The poisonous ones were taken out by means of a long-handled steel hook. All that is necessary to do is to insert this under the snake and lift him off the ground.

===Chinese===

他花费大量时间探究本国毒蛇是否有天敌，并发现巴西许多危险毒蛇最可怕的天敌，竟是一种无毒、完全无害、颇为罕见的巴西蛇——穆苏拉马蛇（mussurama）。医生给我们看的种种趣物中，最有意思的，莫过于让我们亲眼目睹穆苏拉马蛇对付一条危险毒蛇的全过程。
医生先给我们看了浸泡在酒精里的各种重要蛇类标本，有毒的与无毒的都有；接着又给我们看了各种蛇毒和各类抗蛇毒血清的制剂，并赠了我们几份后者，供旅途之用。他已能生产两种截然不同的抗蛇毒血清：一种中和响尾蛇咬伤的剧毒，另一种中和矛头蝮属（Lachecis）各种蛇的毒液。这些毒液彼此略有不同，而且矛头蝮属不同种类的蛇毒之间似乎也有差异；有的毒液近乎无色，有的则是黄色——如雅拉拉卡蛇的毒，我亲眼见过。但关键的差异在于蝮蛇类的这些蛇毒与游蛇类（如眼镜蛇、珊瑚蛇）的蛇毒之间的不同。迄今为止，医生尚未研制出能中和这些游蛇类蛇毒的抗血清。这在巴西实际上无甚大碍，因为巴西的珊瑚蛇只有被人胡乱摆弄、赤裸皮肤暴露于其牙下时才有危险；巴西层出不穷的蛇伤事故与死亡，几乎总是要记在矛头蝮属的几个种类和唯一一种响尾蛇的账上。
最后，医生领我们进讲演室，演示他如何做实验。各种蛇装在房间一侧的箱子里，由一位手艺娴熟、不动声色的助手照料；他摆弄蛇时那份冷静无畏的小心劲儿，与医生本人如出一辙。毒蛇用一把长柄钢钩取出，只需把钩子伸到蛇身下面，将它整个提离地面即可。

===Original===

He is not only unable to escape, but he is unable to strike, for he cannot strike unless coiled so as to give himself support and leverage. The table on which the snakes are laid is fairly large and smooth, differing in no way from an ordinary table.
There were a number of us in the room, including two or three photographers. The doctor first put on the table a nonpoisonous but very vicious and truculent colubrine snake. It struck right and left at us. Then the doctor picked it up, opened its mouth, and showed that it had no fangs, and handed it to me. I also opened its mouth and examined its teeth, and then put it down, whereupon, its temper having been much ruffled, it struck violently at me two or three times. In its action and temper this snake was quite as vicious as the most irritable poisonous snakes. Yet it is entirely harmless. One of the innumerable mysteries of nature which are at present absolutely insoluble is why some snakes should be so vicious and others absolutely placid and good-tempered.
After removing the vicious harmless snake, the doctor warned us to get away from the table, and his attendant put on it, in succession, a very big *Lachecis*﻿—of the kind called bushmaster﻿—and a big rattlesnake. Each coiled menacingly, a formidable brute ready to attack anything that approached. Then the attendant adroitly dropped his iron crook on the neck of each in succession, seized it right behind the head, and held it toward the doctor. The snake’s mouth was in each case wide open, and the great fangs erect and very evident. It would not have been possible to have held an African ring-necked cobra in such fashion, because the ring-neck would have ejected its venom through the fangs into the eyes of the onlookers. There was no danger in this case, and the doctor inserted a shallow glass saucer into the mouth of the snake behind the fangs, permitted it to eject its poison, and then himself squeezed out the remaining poison from the poison-bags through the fangs. From the big *Lachecis* came a large quantity of yellow venom, a liquid which speedily crystallized into a number of minute crystals. The rattlesnake yielded a much less quantity of white venom, which the doctor assured us was far more active than the yellow *Lachecis* venom. Then each snake was returned to its box unharmed.

===Chinese===

这样它不但逃不脱，也咬不着人，因为蛇除非盘成一团借以支撑发力，否则无法出击。放蛇的桌子颇大而光滑，与寻常桌子毫无二致。
房间里有我们一行多人，还有两三位摄影师。医生先往桌上放了一条无毒但非常凶悍暴戾的游蛇，它朝我们左咬右咬。医生随即把它拎起来，掰开它的嘴，表明它并无毒牙，然后递到我手里。我也掰开它的嘴查验了它的牙，然后把它放下；它余怒未消，又朝我狠狠咬了两三口。论举动和脾气，这条蛇与最暴躁的毒蛇相比毫不逊色，然而它完全无害。有些蛇为何这般凶悍，有些却绝对温顺和善——这是大自然无数目前全然无解的谜团之一。
把这条凶悍的无毒蛇收走后，医生提醒我们离桌子远些，他的助手随即先后往桌上放了一条非常大的矛头蝮——即所谓布什马斯特蛇——和一条大响尾蛇。两条蛇都威吓地盘起，如凶神恶煞，准备攻击任何靠近的东西。助手随即麻利地把铁钩依次压在每条蛇的颈上，掐住其头后部位，举向医生。两条蛇的嘴都大张着，巨大的毒牙赫然竖起。若是非洲的环颈眼镜蛇，便不能这样抓法，因为环颈蛇会把毒液从毒牙喷射到旁观者的眼睛里。这一回没有危险，医生把一只浅玻璃碟塞进蛇口毒牙之后，让它把毒喷净，再亲手从毒囊里把余毒沿毒牙挤出。那条大矛头蝮产出了大量黄色毒液，很快便凝结成无数细小的晶体；响尾蛇产出的白色毒液少得多，医生向我们保证，其毒性远比矛头蝮的黄毒厉害。然后两条蛇都毫发无损地放回了各自的箱子。

===Original===

After this the doctor took out of a box and presented to me a fine, handsome, nearly black snake, an individual of the species called the mussurama. This is in my eyes perhaps the most interesting serpent in the world. It is a big snake, four or five feet long, sometimes even longer, nearly black, lighter below, with a friendly, placid temper. It lives exclusively on other snakes, and is completely immune to the poison of the *Lachecis* and rattlesnake groups, which contain all the really dangerous snakes of America. Doctor Brazil told me that he had conducted many experiments with this interesting snake. It is not very common, and prefers wet places in which to live. It lays eggs, and the female remains coiled above the eggs, the object being apparently not to warm them, but to prevent too great evaporation. It will not eat when moulting, nor in cold weather. Otherwise it will eat a small snake every five or six days, or a big one every fortnight.
There is the widest difference, both among poisonous and nonpoisonous snakes, not alone in nervousness and irascibility but also in ability to accustom themselves to out-of-the-way surroundings. Many species of nonpoisonous snakes which are entirely harmless, to man or to any other animal except their small prey, are nevertheless very vicious and truculent, striking right and left and biting freely on the smallest provocation﻿—this is the case with the species of which the doctor had previously placed a specimen on the table. Moreover, many snakes, some entirely harmless and some vicious ones, are so nervous and uneasy that it is with the greatest difficulty they can be induced to eat in captivity, and the slightest disturbance or interference will prevent their eating. There are other snakes, however, of which the mussurama is perhaps the best example, which are very good captives, and at the same time very fearless, showing a complete indifference not only to being observed but to being handled when they are feeding.
There is in the United States a beautiful and attractive snake, the king-snake, with much the same habits as the mussurama. It is friendly toward mankind, and not poisonous, so that it can be handled freely. It feeds on other serpents, and will kill a rattlesnake as big as itself, being immune to the rattlesnake venom. Mr.

===Chinese===

之后，医生从一只箱子里取出一条漂亮俊美的近黑色蛇递到我手中——正是所谓穆苏拉马蛇。在我眼里，它也许是世上最有趣的蛇。它是种大蛇，长四五英尺，有时更长，通体近黑，腹面色浅，性情友善平和。它专门以别的蛇为食，对矛头蝮类和响尾蛇类的蛇毒完全免疫——美洲所有真正危险的蛇都在这两类之中。布拉西尔医生告诉我，他对这种有趣的蛇做过许多实验。它并不常见，喜居湿地；它产卵，雌蛇盘踞在卵上，其目的显然不是为卵加温，而是防止水分过度蒸发；蜕皮期间和天冷时它不进食，其余时候每五六天吃一条小蛇，或每两周吃一条大蛇。
无论毒蛇还是无毒蛇，彼此之间差别极大，不仅在于神经质程度和易怒程度，也在于适应陌生环境的能力。许多种无毒蛇，对人或其他任何动物（其小小猎物除外）都全然无害，却非常凶悍暴戾，稍受刺激便左右乱咬——医生先前放到桌上的那种蛇正是如此。此外，许多蛇——有些完全无害，有些凶悍成性——神经极其紧张不安，囚禁之下极难诱其进食，最轻微的惊扰都会令其绝食。然而另有一些蛇——穆苏拉马蛇或许是最好的例子——被囚后依然安之若素，同时又极其无畏，不仅任人观瞧，进食时任人摆弄也全不在乎。
美国有一种美观动人的蛇，叫王蛇（king-snake），习性同穆苏拉马蛇颇为相似。它待人友善，又无毒性，可以随意上手；它以其他蛇类为食，能咬死一条与它同样大的响尾蛇，因为对响尾蛇毒免疫。

===Original===

Ditmars, of the Bronx Zoo, has made many interesting experiments with these king-snakes. I have had them in my own possession. They are good-natured and can generally be handled with impunity, but I have known them to bite, whereas Doctor Brazil informed me that it was almost impossible to make the mussurama bite a man. The king-snake will feed greedily on other snakes in the presence of man﻿—I knew of one case where it partly swallowed another snake while both were in a small boy’s pocket. It is immune to viper poison but it is not immune to colubrine poison. A couple of years ago I was informed of a case where one of these king-snakes was put into an enclosure with an Indian snake-eating cobra or hamadryad of about the same size. It killed the cobra but made no effort to swallow it, and very soon showed the effects of the cobra poison. I believe it afterward died, but unfortunately I have mislaid my notes and cannot now remember the details of the incident.
Doctor Brazil informed me that the mussurama, like the king-snake, was not immune to the colubrine poison. A mussurama in his possession, which had with impunity killed and eaten several rattlesnakes and representatives of the *Lachecis* genus, also killed and ate a venomous coral-snake, but shortly afterward itself died from the effects of the poison. It is one of the many puzzles of nature that these American serpents which kill poisonous serpents should only have grown immune to the poison of the most dangerous American poisonous serpents, the pit-vipers, and should not have become immune to the poison of the coral-snakes which are commonly distributed throughout their range. Yet, judging by the one instance mentioned by Doctor Brazil, they attack and master these coral-snakes, although the conflict in the end results in their death. It would be interesting to find out whether this attack was exceptional, that is, whether the mussurama has or has not as a species learned to avoid the coral-snake. If it was not exceptional, then not only is the instance highly curious in itself, but it would also go far to explain the failure of the mussurama to become plentiful.

===Chinese===

布朗克斯动物园的迪特马先生（Mr. Ditmars）曾对这些王蛇做过许多有趣的实验。我自己也养过它们。它们脾气温和，一般随便摆弄也无妨，但我知道它们也咬人；而布拉西尔医生告诉我，要让穆苏拉马蛇咬人几乎是不可能的。王蛇当着人的面照样大嚼其他蛇——我知道有一个例子，一条王蛇和另一条蛇同在一个小男孩的口袋里时，竟把那条蛇吞下了半截。它对蝰蛇毒免疫，对游蛇毒却不免疫。几年前，我听人说过一个案例：一条这样的王蛇被放进一只围栏，与一条大小相仿的印度食蛇眼镜蛇（即眼镜王蛇）同处。它咬死了那条眼镜蛇，却不曾试图吞食，很快便显出中了蛇毒的样子。我想它后来死了，可惜我的笔记不知放到哪里去了，如今已记不起这件事的细节。
布拉西尔医生告诉我，穆苏拉马蛇同王蛇一样，对游蛇类蛇毒没有免疫力。他养的一条穆苏拉马蛇，此前咬死吃掉过几条响尾蛇和矛头蝮属的蛇而安然无恙，后来又咬死并吞下了一条有毒的珊瑚蛇，但不久便因蛇毒发作而死。这些以毒蛇为食的美洲蛇类，偏偏只对美洲最危险的毒蛇——蝮蛇类——的毒液产生了免疫力，而对遍布其分布范围内的珊瑚蛇的毒液却不免疫——这是大自然的又一难解之谜。然而，从布拉西尔医生提到的那个例子看，它们照样攻击并制服这些珊瑚蛇，尽管搏斗的结局是它们自己死亡。弄清这种攻击是否属于例外，即穆苏拉马蛇这一物种是否学会了回避珊瑚蛇，将是一件有趣的事。倘若并非例外，那么此事不仅本身极为奇特，也足以说明穆苏拉马蛇为何始终繁衍不盛。

===Original===

For the benefit of those who are not acquainted with the subject, I may mention that the poison of a poisonous snake is not dangerous to its own species unless injected in very large doses, about ten times what would normally be injected by a bite; but that it is deadly to all other snakes, poisonous or nonpoisonous, save as regards the very few species which themselves eat poisonous snakes. The Indian hamadryad, or giant cobra, is exclusively a snake-eater. It evidently draws a sharp distinction between poisonous and nonpoisonous snakes, for Mr. Ditmars has recorded that two individuals in the Bronx Zoo which are habitually fed on harmless snakes, and attack them eagerly, refused to attack a copperhead which was thrown into their cage, being evidently afraid of this pit-viper. It would be interesting to find out if the hamadryad is afraid to prey on all pit-vipers, and also whether it will prey on its small relative, the true cobra﻿—for it may well be that, even if not immune to the viper poison, it is immune to the poison of its close ally, the smaller cobra.
All these and many other questions would be speedily settled by Doctor Brazil if he were given the opportunity to test them. It must be remembered, moreover, that not only have his researches been of absorbing value from the standpoint of pure science but that they also have a real utilitarian worth. He is now collecting and breeding the mussurama. The favorite prey of the mussurama is the most common and therefore the most dangerous poisonous snake of Brazil, the jararaca, which is known in Martinique as the fer-de-lance. In Martinique and elsewhere this snake is such an object of terror as to be at times a genuine scourge. Surely it would be worth while for the authorities of Martinique to import specimens of the mussurama to that island. The mortality from snakebite in British India is very great. Surely it would be well worth while for the able Indian Government to copy Brazil and create such an institute as that over which Doctor Vital Brazil is the curator.
At first sight it seems extraordinary that poisonous serpents, so dreaded by and so irresistible to most animals, should be so utterly helpless before the few creatures that prey on them. But the explanation is easy.

===Chinese===

为让不熟悉这一问题的读者明白，不妨说明：毒蛇的毒液对其本种并不危险，除非注入极大剂量——约为一次咬击正常注入量的十倍；但对所有其他蛇类，无论有毒无毒，都是致命的，只有少数几种本身以毒蛇为食的除外。印度的眼镜王蛇（hamadryad，即巨眼镜蛇）专以蛇为食。它显然对有毒与无毒之蛇分得一清二楚：迪特马先生记录过，布朗克斯动物园有两条惯以无害之蛇喂养、见蛇就扑的眼镜王蛇，面对投入笼中的一条铜头蝮（copperhead）却拒不出击，分明是惧怕这条蝮蛇。弄清眼镜王蛇是否害怕捕食一切蝮蛇，以及它是否捕食自己那位较小的近亲——真正的眼镜蛇——也很有意思；因为很可能它即使对蝰蛇毒不免疫，却对其近亲小眼镜蛇的毒液免疫。
这一切以及许多别的问题，只要给布拉西尔医生验证的机会，都能很快解决。况且应当记住，他的研究不仅从纯科学角度看具有引人入胜的价值，还有实实在在的实用价值。他目前正着手搜集、繁育穆苏拉马蛇。穆苏拉马蛇最爱吃的猎物，是巴西最常见、因而也最危险的毒蛇——雅拉拉卡蛇，在马提尼克被称为枪头蛇。在马提尼克等地，这种蛇令人谈之色变，有时真成了一方之祸。马提尼克当局若引进若干穆苏拉马蛇到该岛，想必是值得的。英属印度死于蛇咬的人数极多，精明的印度政府若效法巴西，也设立一座维塔尔·布拉西尔医生所主持的那样的研究所，想必非常值得。
乍看之下似乎很奇怪：毒蛇令大多数动物闻风丧胆、无可抵挡，在少数以它们为食的动物面前却全然无措。但解释起来并不难。

===Original===

Any highly specialized creature, the higher its specialization, is apt to be proportionately helpless when once its peculiar specialized traits are effectively nullified by an opponent. This is eminently the case with the most dangerous poisonous snakes. In them a highly peculiar specialization has been carried to the highest point. They rely for attack and defence purely on their poison-fangs. All other means and methods of attack and defence have atrophied. They neither crush nor tear with their teeth nor constrict with their bodies. The poison-fangs are slender and delicate, and, save for the poison, the wound inflicted is of a trivial character. In consequence they are helpless in the presence of any animal which the poison does not affect. There are several mammals immune to snakebite, including various species of hedgehog, pig, and mongoose﻿—the other mammals which kill them do so by pouncing on them unawares or by avoiding their stroke through sheer quickness of movement; and probably this is the case with most snake-eating birds. The mongoose is very quick, but in some cases at least﻿—I have mentioned one in the “African Game Trails”﻿—it permits itself to be bitten by poisonous snakes, treating the bite with utter indifference. There should be extensive experiments made to determine if there are species of mongoose immune to both cobra and viper poison. Hedgehogs, as determined by actual experiments, pay no heed at all to viper poison even when bitten on such tender places as the tongue and lips and eat the snake as if it were a radish. Even among animals which are not immune to the poison different species are very differently affected by the different kinds of snake poisons. Not only are some species more resistant than others to all poisons, but there is a wide variation in the amount of immunity each displays to any given venom. One species will be quickly killed by the poison from one species of snake, and be fairly resistant to the poison of another; whereas in another species the conditions may be directly reversed.
The mussurama which Doctor Brazil handed me was a fine specimen, perhaps four and a half feet long.

===Chinese===

任何高度特化的生物，特化程度越高，一旦它独特的特化本领被对手有效化解，便越是无能为力。最危险的毒蛇正是这方面的突出例子：在它们身上，一种极为独特的特化被推到了顶点。它们攻守全凭毒牙，其他一切攻防手段都退化了；它们既不用牙咬碎撕裂，也不用身体缠绞；毒牙纤细脆弱，若无毒液，所造成的创口无足轻重。因此，面对任何不受其毒液影响的动物，它们便束手无策。有几种哺乳动物对蛇咬免疫，包括各种刺猬、猪和獴——其余那些能杀蛇的哺乳动物，靠的是出其不意地扑击，或全凭身手迅捷躲开蛇的出击；大多数食蛇鸟类大概也是如此。獴极其敏捷，但至少在某些情况下——我在《非洲猎踪》（African Game Trails）里提到过一个例子——它任凭毒蛇咬自己，对咬伤全然不当回事。应当作广泛的实验，查明是否有对眼镜蛇毒和蝰蛇毒都免疫的獴。实验证明，刺猬对蝰蛇毒全不在乎，即便舌头和嘴唇这样娇嫩的部位被咬，也照样把蛇当萝卜似地吃掉。
即便在对蛇毒不免疫的动物中，不同种类受不同蛇毒的影响也大不相同。不仅有些种类比其他种类对各种毒液都更有抵抗力，而且每种动物对任何特定毒液所显示的免疫程度也差别悬殊：有的动物会被一种蛇的毒液迅速杀死，却对另一种的毒液颇有抵抗力；换一种动物，情况可能恰好相反。
布拉西尔医生递给我的那条穆苏拉马蛇是上好的样本，约有四英尺半长。

===Original===

I lifted the smooth, lithe bulk in my hands, and then let it twist its coils so that it rested at ease in my arms; it glided to and fro, on its own length, with the sinuous grace of its kind, and showed not the slightest trace of either nervousness or bad temper. Meanwhile the doctor bade his attendant put on the table a big jararaca, or fer-de-lance, which was accordingly done. The jararaca was about three feet and a half, or perhaps nearly four feet long﻿—that is, it was about nine inches shorter than the mussurama. The latter, which I continued to hold in my arms, behaved with friendly and impassive indifference, moving easily to and fro through my hands, and once or twice hiding its head between the sleeve and the body of my coat. The doctor was not quite sure how the mussurama would behave, for it had recently eaten a small snake, and unless hungry it pays no attention whatever to venomous snakes, even when they attack and bite it. However, it fortunately proved still to have a good appetite.
The jararaca was alert and vicious. It partly coiled itself on the table, threatening the bystanders. I put the big black serpent down on the table four or five feet from the enemy and headed in its direction. As soon as I let go with my hands it glided toward where the threatening, formidable-looking lance-head lay stretched in a half coil. The mussurama displayed not the slightest sign of excitement. Apparently it trusted little to its eyes, for it began to run its head along the body of the jararaca, darting out its flickering tongue to feel just where it was, as it nosed its way up toward the head of its antagonist. So placid were its actions that I did not at first suppose that it meant to attack, for there was not the slightest exhibition of anger or excitement.
It was the jararaca that began the fight. It showed no fear whatever of its foe, but its irritable temper was aroused by the proximity and actions of the other, and like a flash it drew back its head and struck, burying its fangs in the forward part of the mussurama’s body. Immediately the latter struck in return, and the counterattack was so instantaneous that it was difficult to see just what had happened.

===Chinese===

我把这条光滑柔韧的大家伙托在手里，由它自行盘绕，安然伏在我臂弯里；它沿着自己的身子来回游动，姿态婉转，一如其类，看不出丝毫紧张或坏脾气的迹象。与此同时，医生吩咐助手把一条大雅拉拉卡蛇（即枪头蛇）放到桌上，助手依言照办。这条雅拉拉卡蛇约三英尺半长，或者将近四英尺——也就是说，比穆苏拉马蛇短九英寸左右。后者仍由我抱在怀里，举止友善而木然，任由它在我手中从容游走，有一两次还把头藏进我外衣的袖子与衣身之间。医生起初拿不准穆苏拉马蛇会作何反应，因为它不久前刚吃过一条小蛇；这种蛇不饿的时候对毒蛇毫不理会，即便毒蛇攻击、撕咬它也不还手。所幸它胃口依然很好。
那条雅拉拉卡蛇警惕而凶悍，在桌上半盘起身子，威吓旁观的人。我把这条黑色大蛇放到桌上离敌蛇四五英尺处，头朝着对方。我一撒手，它便朝那凶相毕露、盘作半圈的矛头滑去。穆苏拉马蛇没有显出丝毫兴奋。它显然不大相信自己的眼睛，先把头贴着雅拉拉卡蛇的身子游走，不时吐出颤动的信子探明对方位置，一路循着对手的头嗅探而上。它的举动如此安详，起初我并不以为它要出击，因为看不到一点愤怒或激动的表示。
先动手的是雅拉拉卡蛇。它对敌手毫无惧色，但对方的逼近和举动激起了它的火性；它倏地缩头出击，毒牙深深扎进穆苏拉马蛇身体前部。穆苏拉马蛇随即还击，反击来得如此迅疾，一时很难看清发生了什么。

===Original===

There was tremendous writhing and struggling on the part of the jararaca; and then, leaning over the knot into which the two serpents were twisted, I saw that the mussurama had seized the jararaca by the lower jaw, putting its own head completely into the wide-gaping mouth of the poisonous snake. The long fangs were just above the top of the mussurama’s head; and it appeared, as well as I could see, that they were once again driven into the mussurama; but without the slightest effect. Then the fangs were curved back in the jaw, a fact which I particularly noted, and all effort at the offensive was abandoned by the poisonous snake.
Meanwhile the mussurama was chewing hard, and gradually shifted its grip, little by little, until it got the top of the head of the jararaca in its mouth, the lower jaw of the jararaca being spread out to one side. The venomous serpent was helpless; the fearsome master of the wild life of the forest, the deadly foe of humankind, was itself held in the grip of death. Its cold, baleful serpent’s eyes shone, as evil as ever. But it was dying. In vain it writhed and struggled. Nothing availed it.
Once or twice the mussurama took a turn round the middle of the body of its opponent, but it did not seem to press hard, and apparently used its coils chiefly in order to get a better grip so as to crush the head of its antagonist, or to hold the latter in place. This crushing was done by its teeth; and the repeated bites were made with such effort that the muscles stood out on the mussurama’s neck. Then it took two coils round the neck of the jararaca and proceeded deliberately to try to break the backbone of its opponent by twisting the head round. With this purpose it twisted its own head and neck round so that the lighter-colored surface was uppermost; and indeed at one time it looked as if it had made almost a complete single spiral revolution of its own body. It never for a moment relaxed its grip except to shift slightly the jaws.
In a few minutes the jararaca was dead, its head crushed in, although the body continued to move convulsively. When satisfied that its opponent was dead, the mussurama began to try to get the head in its mouth. This was a process of some difficulty on account of the angle at which the lower jaw of the jararaca stuck out. But finally the head was taken completely inside and then swallowed.

===Chinese===

雅拉拉卡蛇拼命翻腾挣扎；随后我俯身细看那两条绞作一团的蛇，见穆苏拉马蛇已咬住雅拉拉卡蛇的下颌，把自己的头完全伸进了那条毒蛇大张的嘴里。那对长毒牙正悬在穆苏拉马蛇头顶上方；就我所能看清的，毒牙又一次刺进了穆苏拉马蛇的身体，却没有产生丝毫效果。接着毒牙向后收拢回颌中——这一节我特意留意了——那条毒蛇彻底放弃了进攻。
穆苏拉马蛇这边则大嚼特嚼，一点点挪移钳制之处，最后把雅拉拉卡蛇的头顶衔进口中，雅拉拉卡蛇的下颌被撇向一边。毒蛇束手就擒了；这林中生灵的主宰、人类的死敌，此刻自己落入了死亡的钳制。它那双阴冷的蛇眼依旧闪着凶光。但它正在死去。它翻腾挣扎，终归徒劳，一切都救不了它。
穆苏拉马蛇有一两次在对手身体中段绕上一圈，但似乎并不收紧，缠绞显然主要是为了取得更好的钳制位置，好咬碎对手的头，或将其固定在原地。碾碎全凭牙齿；一次次咬合用力之猛，穆苏拉马蛇颈上的肌肉都凸了起来。接着它在雅拉拉卡蛇颈上绕了两圈，从容不迫地扭转蛇头，要把对手的脊椎折断。为此它把自己的头颈也拧转过去，浅色的腹面朝上；有一阵子看去竟像它把自己的身子几乎整整旋转了一圈。除了微微调整颌的位置，它一刻也不曾松口。
几分钟后雅拉拉卡蛇便死了，头已被咬碎，身子却还在抽搐。确认对手已死后，穆苏拉马蛇开始设法把它的头衔进嘴里。由于雅拉拉卡蛇的下颌支棱着一个角度，这一步骤颇为费事，但最终那颗头被整个含了进去，随后吞下。

===Original===

After this, the mussurama proceeded deliberately, but with unbroken speed, to devour its opponent by the simple process of crawling outside it, the body and tail of the jararaca writhing and struggling until the last. During the early portion of the meal, the mussurama put a stop to this writhing and struggling by resting its own body on that of its prey; but toward the last the part of the body that remained outside was left free to wriggle as it wished.
Not only was the mussurama totally indifferent to our presence, but it was totally indifferent to being handled while the meal was going on. Several times I replaced the combatants in the middle of the table when they had writhed to the edge, and finally, when the photographers found that they could not get good pictures, I held the mussurama up against a white background with the partially swallowed snake in its mouth; and the feast went on uninterruptedly. I never saw cooler or more utterly unconcerned conduct; and the ease and certainty with which the terrible poisonous snake was mastered gave me the heartiest respect and liking for the easygoing, good-natured, and exceedingly efficient serpent which I had been holding in my arms.
---
Our trip was not intended as a hunting-trip but as a scientific expedition. Before starting on the trip itself, while travelling in the Argentine, I received certain pieces of firsthand information concerning the natural history of the jaguar, and of the cougar, or puma, which are worth recording. The facts about the jaguar are not new in the sense of casting new light on its character, although they are interesting; but the facts about the behavior of the puma in one district of Patagonia are of great interest, because they give an entirely new side of its life-history.
There was travelling with me at the time Doctor Francisco P. Moreno, of Buenos Aires. Doctor Moreno is at the present day a member of the National Board of Education of the Argentine, a man who has worked in every way for the benefit of his country, perhaps especially for the benefit of the children, so that when he was first introduced to me it was as the “Jacob Riis of the Argentine”﻿—for they know my deep and affectionate intimacy with Jacob Riis. He is also an eminent man of science, who has done admirable work as a geologist and a geographer.

===Chinese===

此后，穆苏拉马蛇从容而不间断地，用从猎物体外爬行而过的简单办法吞食对手；雅拉拉卡蛇的躯干和尾巴直到最后一刻还在翻腾挣扎。吞食的前半程，穆苏拉马蛇把身子压在猎物身上，止住了它的翻腾挣扎；到后半程，留在外面的那截身子便任其自扭自动了。
穆苏拉马蛇不仅对我们的在场全然无动于衷，进食时任人摆弄也毫不在乎。有几回两条蛇翻腾到桌边，我把它们挪回桌子中央；最后，摄影师们发现拍不好照片，我便举着穆苏拉马蛇对着白色背景，让它口衔半吞的蛇——这场盛宴照常进行，毫无间断。我从未见过比这更冷静、更满不在乎的举止；那条可怕毒蛇被制服得如此轻易而确定，使我对怀里抱过的这条随和、善良、本领高强的蛇，油然生出由衷的敬意与好感。
---
我们的此行并非狩猎之旅，而是一次科学考察。在正式启程之前、于阿根廷旅行期间，我获得了一些关于美洲豹（jaguar）和美洲狮（cougar，或称puma）博物学的第一手资料，值得记录下来。关于美洲豹的这些事实虽有趣，但并不新鲜，谈不上对其本性有新的揭示；而关于美洲狮在巴塔哥尼亚某一地区行为的事实却极有价值，因为它们展示了这种动物生命史中全然不同的一面。
当时与我同行的是布宜诺斯艾利斯的弗朗西斯科·P·莫雷诺医生（Doctor Moreno）。莫雷诺医生如今是阿根廷国家教育委员会的成员，一位以各种方式为国效劳的人，或许尤其为儿童谋福祉，因此初次介绍给我时，人称他是“阿根廷的雅各布·里斯”——因为他们知道我与雅各布·里斯交谊深厚。他也是一位杰出的科学人士，作为地质学家和地理学家均有令人称道的建树。

===Original===

At one period, in connection with his duties as a boundary commissioner on the survey between Chile and the Argentine, he worked for years in Patagonia. It was he who made the extraordinary discovery in a Patagonian cave of the still fresh fragments of skin and other remains of the mylodon, the aberrant horse known as the *Onohippidium*, the huge South American tiger, and the *Macrauchenia*, all of them extinct animals. This discovery showed that some of the strange representatives of the giant South American Pleistocene fauna had lasted down to within a comparatively few thousand years, down to the time when man, substantially as the Spaniards found him, flourished on the continent. Incidentally the discovery tended to show that this fauna had lasted much later in South America than was the case with the corresponding faunas in other parts of the world; and therefore it tended to disprove the claims advanced by Doctor Ameghino for the extreme age, geologically, of this fauna, and for the extreme antiquity of man on the American continent.
One day Doctor Moreno handed me a copy of *The Outlook* containing my account of a cougar-hunt in Arizona, saying that he noticed that I had very little faith in cougars attacking men, although I had explicitly stated that such attacks sometimes occurred. I told him, Yes, that I had found that the cougar was practically harmless to man, the undoubtedly authentic instances of attacks on men being so exceptional that they could in practice be wholly disregarded. Thereupon Doctor Moreno showed me a scar on his face, and told me that he had himself been attacked and badly mauled by a puma which was undoubtedly trying to prey on him; that is, which had started on a career as a man-eater. This was to me most interesting. I had often met men who knew other men who had seen other men who said that they had been attacked by pumas, but this was the first time that I had ever come across a man who had himself been attacked. Doctor Moreno, as I have said, is not only an eminent citizen, but an eminent scientific man, and his account of what occurred is unquestionably a scientifically accurate statement of the facts. I give it exactly as the doctor told it; paraphrasing a letter he sent me, and including one or two answers to questions I put to him. The doctor, by the way, stated to me that he had known Mr.

===Chinese===

有一段时期，他因担任智利与阿根廷划界委员的职务，在巴塔哥尼亚工作多年。正是在一个巴塔哥尼亚洞穴里，他作出过惊人的发现：大地懒（mylodon）、被称为滑距骨马的奇异马型动物（Onohippidium）、巨大的南美虎，以及后弓兽（Macrauchenia）的皮肉残片与其他遗骸，尚且新鲜——这些动物全都已灭绝。这一发现表明，南美巨型更新世动物群中某些奇特的孑遗，一直存续到距今不过几千年的时期，存续到人类——与后来西班牙人所见者大体相同的人类——在这片大陆上繁衍生息的时代。顺带一提，这一发现还表明，这一动物群在南美洲延续的时间远比世界其他地区相应的动物群晚；因此它也趋于否定阿梅吉诺医生（Doctor Ameghino）所主张的这一动物群在地质上极为古老、以及人类在美洲大陆生存年代极为久远的说法。
一天，莫雷诺医生递给我一期《瞭望》（The Outlook）杂志，里面有我写的亚利桑那猎狮记；他说他注意到，我虽然明确说过美洲狮有时确实会袭人，但骨子里并不相信美洲狮会攻击人。我回答说是的：据我所见，美洲狮对人几乎无害，袭击人的确凿事例实在凤毛麟角，实际中完全可以置之不理。于是莫雷诺医生给我看脸上的疤，说他本人就被一头美洲狮袭击过，伤得不轻，而那头狮子无疑是想拿他当猎物；也就是说，它已开始走上食人之路。这在我看来极为有趣。我常常遇到这样的人：他们认识别的人，别的人又见过另一些人，另一些人说自己被美洲狮袭击过；但亲身遭袭的人，这还是头一回遇到。如前所述，莫雷诺医生不仅是一位杰出公民，还是一位卓有成就的科学人士，他对事件的叙述无疑是科学上准确的事实陈述。我把他的讲述原原本本录在这里，改写自他写给我的一封信，并附上我向他提问后他的一两处答复。顺带一提，医生还对我说起，他认识

===Original===

Hudson, the author of the *Naturalist on the Plata*, and that the latter knew nothing whatever of pumas from personal experience and had accepted as facts utterly wild fables.
Undoubtedly, said the doctor, the puma in South America, like the puma in North America, is, as a general rule, a cowardly animal which not only never attacks man, but rarely makes any efficient defence when attacked. The Indian and white hunters have no fear of it in most parts of the country, and its harmlessness to man is proverbial. But there is one particular spot in southern Patagonia where cougars, to the doctor’s own personal knowledge, have for years been dangerous foes of man. This curious local change in habits, by the way, is nothing unprecedented as regards wild animals. In portions of its range, as I am informed by Mr. Lord Smith, the Asiatic tiger can hardly be forced to fight man, and never preys on him, while throughout most of its range it is a most dangerous beast, and often turns man-eater. So there are waters in which sharks are habitual man-eaters, and others where they never touch men; and there are rivers and lakes where crocodiles or caymans are very dangerous, and others where they are practically harmless﻿—I have myself seen this in Africa.
In March, 1877, Doctor Moreno with a party of men working on the boundary commission, and with a number of Patagonian horse-Indians, was encamped for some weeks beside Lake Viedma, which had not before been visited by white men for a century, and which was rarely visited even by Indians. One morning, just before sunrise, he left his camp by the south shore of the lake, to make a topographical sketch of the lake. He was unarmed, but carried a prismatic compass in a leather case with a strap. It was cold, and he wrapped his poncho of guanaco-hide round his neck and head. He had walked a few hundred yards, when a puma, a female, sprang on him from behind and knocked him down. As she sprang on him she tried to seize his head with one paw, striking him on the shoulder with the other. She lacerated his mouth and also his back, but tumbled over with him, and in the scuffle they separated before she could bite him. He sprang to his feet, and, as he said, was forced to think quickly.

===Chinese===

《拉普拉塔河畔的博物学家》（Naturalist on the Plata）的作者哈德逊先生（Mr. Hudson），并说后者对美洲狮毫无亲身经验，却把荒诞不经的传说当成了事实。
医生说，毫无疑问，南美的美洲狮同北美的美洲狮一样，总体而言是一种怯懦的动物，不仅从不攻击人，遇袭时也很少能作出有效的自卫。在这个国家的大部分地区，印第安猎人和白人猎人都不怕它，它之不伤人是出了名的。但在巴塔哥尼亚南部有一处特别的地方，医生亲知亲见，那里的美洲狮多年来一直是人的危险敌人。顺带一提，这种古怪的习性因地而变，在野兽之中并非没有先例。据洛德·史密斯先生相告，亚洲虎在其分布范围的部分地区几乎无法被激得与人搏斗，从不以人为食；而在其分布范围的大部分地区，它却是最危险的猛兽，常常变为食人兽。同样，有些水域里的鲨鱼惯于食人，另一些水域里的鲨鱼却从不伤人；有些河流湖泊里的鳄鱼或凯门鳄非常危险，另一些里的却几乎无害——这我在非洲亲眼见过。
1877年3月，莫雷诺医生带着划界委员会的一队工作人员和一群巴塔哥尼亚马背印第安人，在别德马湖畔扎营数周。此前一个世纪里，白人从未到过此湖，就连印第安人也罕至。一天清晨，天将破晓，他离开湖南岸的营地，去给湖画一幅地形草图。他没有带武器，只带着一只装在皮套里、系着背带的棱镜罗盘。天气寒冷，他把原驼皮的斗篷裹在脖子和头上。走出几百码后，一头美洲狮——一头母狮——从背后向他扑来，把他扑倒在地。扑击之际，它一只爪子抓向他的头，另一只打在他肩上。他的嘴和背都被抓伤，但它也随他一同翻滚，乱作一团中还没来得及下口，两者便分开了。他一跃而起，用他自己的话说，不得不急中生智。

===Original===

She had recovered herself, and sat on her haunches like a cat, looking at him, and then crouched to spring again; whereupon he whipped off his poncho, and as she sprang at him he opened it, and at the same moment hit her head with the prismatic compass in its case which he held by the strap. She struck the poncho and was evidently puzzled by it, for, turning, she slunk off to one side, under a bush, and then proceeded to try to get round behind him. He faced her, keeping his eyes upon her, and backed off. She followed him for three or four hundred yards. At least twice she came up to attack him, but each time he opened his poncho and yelled, and at the last moment she shrank back. She continually, however, tried, by taking advantage of cover, to sneak up to one side, or behind, to attack him. Finally, when he got near camp, she abandoned the pursuit and went into a small patch of bushes. He raised the alarm; an Indian rode up and set fire to the bushes from the windward side. When the cougar broke from the bushes, the Indian rode after her, and threw his bolas, which twisted around her hind legs; and while she was struggling to free herself, he brained her with his second bolas. The doctor’s injuries were rather painful, but not serious.
Twenty-one years later, in April, 1898, he was camped on the same lake, but on the north shore, at the foot of a basaltic cliff. He was in company with four soldiers, with whom he had travelled from the Strait of Magellan. In the night he was aroused by the shriek of a man and the barking of his dogs. As the men sprang up from where they were lying asleep they saw a large puma run off out of the firelight into the darkness. It had sprung on a soldier named Marcelino Huquen while he was asleep, and had tried to carry him off. Fortunately, the man was so wrapped up in his blanket, as the night was cold, that he was not injured. The puma was never found or killed.
About the same time a surveyor of Doctor Moreno’s party, a Swede named Arneberg, was attacked in similar fashion. The doctor was not with him at the time. Mr. Arneberg was asleep in the forest near Lake San Martin. The cougar both bit and clawed him, and tore his mouth, breaking out three teeth. The man was rescued; but this puma also escaped.

===Chinese===

母狮已站稳身子，像猫一样蹲坐起来，盯着他，随即又伏身欲扑；他猛地扯下斗篷，趁它扑来时将斗篷张开，同时抡起手提皮套的棱镜罗盘，砸在它头上。它扑在斗篷上，显然被弄糊涂了，转身溜到一边的灌木丛下，接着便设法绕到他背后去。他正面对着它，眼睛紧盯不放，一步步退走。它跟着他走了三四百码。它至少两回逼近要攻击他，但每回他都张开斗篷大吼，它临了又缩了回去。但它一直借着掩体，想悄悄溜到他侧翼或身后下手。最后，等他快到营地时，它放弃追赶，钻进了一小片灌木丛。他高声示警；一名印第安人骑马赶来，从上风头点燃了灌木丛。美洲狮从灌木丛中窜出时，那印第安人策马追赶，掷出流星索，缠住了它的后腿；趁它挣扎脱身之际，他又抡起第二条流星索，砸碎了它的脑袋。医生的伤相当疼，但不严重。
二十一年后，即1898年4月，他又在同一片湖畔扎营，不过是在北岸，一处玄武岩峭壁脚下。同行的有四名士兵，是他从麦哲伦海峡一路带着来的。夜里，一声男人的惨叫和狗吠把他惊醒。众人从睡处跳起时，只见一头大美洲狮逃出火光，窜入黑暗。它扑在一名叫马塞利诺·乌肯的士兵身上，当时那人正在酣睡，它想把他叼走。所幸夜里天冷，那人裹得严严实实，竟未受伤。那头美洲狮始终没有找到，也没有被打死。
大约同一时期，莫雷诺医生队伍里的一名测量员——一个名叫阿内贝里的瑞典人——也遭到过类似的袭击。当时医生不和他在一起。阿内贝里先生睡在圣马丁湖附近的森林里。美洲狮又咬又抓，撕破了他的嘴，打掉了三颗牙。人被救了下来；这头美洲狮也逃掉了。

===Original===

The doctor stated that in this particular locality the Indians, who elsewhere paid no heed whatever to the puma, never let their women go out after wood for fuel unless two or three were together. This was because on several occasions women who had gone out alone were killed by pumas. Evidently in this one locality the habit of at least occasional man-eating has become chronic with a species which elsewhere is the most cowardly, and to man the least dangerous, of all the big cats.
These observations of Doctor Moreno have a peculiar value, because, as far as I know, they are the first trustworthy accounts of a cougar’s having attacked man save under circumstances so exceptional as to make the attack signify little more than the similar exceptional instances of attack by various other species of wild animals that are not normally dangerous to man.
The jaguar, however, has long been known not only to be a dangerous foe when itself attacked, but also now and then to become a man-eater. Therefore the instances of such attacks furnished me are of merely corroborative value.
In the excellent zoological gardens at Buenos Aires the curator, Doctor Onelli, a naturalist of note, showed us a big male jaguar which had been trapped in the Chaco, where it had already begun a career as a man-eater, having killed three persons. They were killed, and two of them were eaten; the animal was trapped, in consequence of the alarm excited by the death of his third victim. This jaguar was very savage; whereas a young jaguar, which was in a cage with a young tiger, was playful and friendly, as was also the case with the young tiger. On my trip to visit La Plata Museum I was accompanied by Captain Vicente Montes, of the Argentine Navy, an accomplished officer of scientific attainments. He had at one time been engaged on a survey of the boundary between the Argentine and Paraná and Brazil. They had a quantity of dried beef in camp. On several occasions a jaguar came into camp after this dried beef. Finally they succeeded in protecting it so that he could not reach it. The result, however, was disastrous. On the next occasion that he visited camp, at midnight, he seized a man. Everybody was asleep at the time, and the jaguar came in so noiselessly as to elude the vigilance of the dogs.

===Chinese===

医生说，在这个特定的地方，印第安人——他们在别处对美洲狮全然不放在心上——从不让女人单独出去打柴，非得两三人同行不可。因为好几回，单独外出的女人都被美洲狮咬死了。显然，单单在这一地区，这个在别处最怯懦、对人最无害的大型猫类，至少偶一为之的食人习性竟成了积习。
莫雷诺医生的这些观察之所以有特殊价值，是因为据我所知，它们是关于美洲狮攻击人的头一批可信记述——此前的事例都发生在极端特殊的情形之下，其意义无非同其他种种通常不伤人的野兽偶发袭人一样，说明不了什么。
而美洲豹（jaguar）则早已为人所知：它不仅受攻击时是个危险的对手，偶尔还会变成食人兽。因此别人向我提供的此类袭人事例，只是印证性的价值。
在布宜诺斯艾利斯那座出色的动物园里，园长、知名博物学家奥内利医生领我们看了一头雄性大美洲豹。它是在查科（the Chaco）落网的，在那里已经干起了食人的营生，咬死过三个人。三人都被咬死，其中两人还被吃掉；正因为第三名死者引起恐慌，这头豹子才被捉住。这头美洲豹非常凶猛；而同笼的一头幼豹与一头幼虎在一起，却活泼友善，那头幼虎也是如此。我去参观拉普拉塔博物馆时，阿根廷海军的维森特·蒙特斯上尉与我同行——他是位有科学造诣的干练军官。他曾参与阿根廷与巴拉那及巴西之间边界的勘测。他们在营地里存着大量牛肉干。有好几回，一头美洲豹为着这牛肉干闯进营地。最后他们终于把肉干护得严严实实，叫它够不着了。结果却招了祸：它下一回再到营地来时，是在半夜，叼住了一个男人。当时众人都在熟睡，豹子进来的动静之小，连狗的警惕都瞒过了。

===Original===

As he seized the man, the latter gave one yell, but the next moment was killed, the jaguar driving his fangs through the man’s skull into the brain. There was a scene of uproar and confusion, and the jaguar was forced to drop his prey and flee into the woods. Next morning they followed him with the dogs, and finally killed him. He was a large male, in first-class condition. The only features of note about these two incidents was that in each case the man-eater was a powerful animal in the prime of life; whereas it frequently happens that the jaguars that turn man-eaters are old animals, and have become too inactive or too feeble to catch their ordinary prey.
---
During the two months before starting from Asuncion, in Paraguay, for our journey into the interior, I was kept so busy that I had scant time to think of natural history. But in a strange land a man who cares for wild birds and wild beasts always sees and hears something that is new to him and interests him. In the dense tropical woods near Rio de Janeiro I heard in late October﻿—springtime, near the southern tropic﻿—the songs of many birds that I could not identify. But the most beautiful music was from a shy woodland thrush, sombre-colored, which lived near the ground in the thick timber, but sang high among the branches. At a great distance we could hear the ringing, musical, bell-like note, long-drawn and of piercing sweetness, which occurs at intervals in the song; at first I thought this was the song, but when it was possible to approach the singer I found that these far-sounding notes were scattered through a continuous song of great melody. I never listened to one that impressed me more. In different places in Argentina I heard and saw the Argentine mockingbird, which is not very unlike our own, and is also a delightful and remarkable singer. But I never heard the wonderful white-banded mockingbird, which is said by Hudson, who knew well the birds of both South America and Europe, to be the song-king of them all.
Most of the birds I thus noticed while hurriedly passing through the country were, of course, the conspicuous ones. The spurred lapwings, big, tame, boldly marked plover, were everywhere; they were very noisy and active and both inquisitive and daring, and they have a very curious dance custom. No man need look for them.

===Chinese===

被叼住的人喊了一声，下一刻便死了——豹子的利牙穿透他的颅骨，直入脑髓。一时人声鼎沸、乱作一团，豹子只得丢下猎物，逃进树林。次日清晨，他们带着猎狗追踪，终于把它杀死了。它是头雄性大豹，膘肥体壮，正是巅峰状态。这两起事件唯一值得注意之处在于：食人的都是正当壮年、体魄强健的猛兽；而美洲豹变为食人兽的，往往是老兽，动作迟缓或体衰力竭，已捉不住惯常的猎物。
---
从巴拉圭的亚松森启程深入内陆之前的两个月里，我忙得不可开交，几乎无暇顾及博物学。但在一个陌生的国度，只要心里装着野鸟野兽，总能看到听到些新鲜的、令人感兴趣的东西。在里约热内卢附近茂密的热带树林里，十月下旬——南回归线附近正值春天——我听到许多鸟的鸣啭，却辨认不出是些什么鸟。但最美的歌声来自一种羞怯的林鸫，羽色黯淡，栖息在密林的下层，却高唱于枝头。隔着老远，我们就能听见那清越、乐音般的、铃铛似的音调，悠长而甜美入骨，在歌声中时时响起；起初我以为这就是它的歌，待到能靠近歌者，才发现这些远闻的音符只是散布在一支旋律连绵的歌里。我听过的鸟歌中，没有一支比它更令我难忘。在阿根廷的不同地方，我听到也看到了阿根廷嘲鸫，同我们北美的嘲鸫颇为相像，也是位讨人喜欢、本领高强的歌手。但我始终无缘一听那奇妙的白带嘲鸫——深谙南美与欧洲鸟类的哈德逊说，它是众鸟之中的歌王。
我匆匆过境时注意到的鸟，当然大多是显眼的那些。带距的麦鸡类——体型大、不怕人、花纹醒目的鸻鸟——到处都是；它们聒噪好动，既好奇又胆大，还有一种非常古怪的舞蹈习俗。人根本不必去找它们。

===Original===

They will look for him, and when they find him they will fairly yell the discovery to the universe. In the marshes of the lower Paraná I saw flocks of scarlet-headed blackbirds on the tops of the reeds; the females are as strikingly colored as the males, and their jet-black bodies and brilliant red heads make it impossible for them to escape observation among their natural surroundings. On the plains to the west I saw flocks of the beautiful rose-breasted starlings; unlike the redheaded blackbirds, which seemed fairly to court attention, these starlings sought to escape observation by crouching on the ground so that their red breasts were hidden. There were yellow-shouldered blackbirds in wet places, and cow-buntings abounded.
But the most conspicuous birds I saw were members of the family of tyrant flycatchers, of which our own kingbird is the most familiar example. This family is very numerously represented in Argentina, both in species and individuals. Some of the species are so striking, both in color and habits, and in one case also in shape, as to attract the attention of even the unobservant. The least conspicuous, and nevertheless very conspicuous, among those that I saw was the bientevido, which is brown above, yellow beneath, with a boldly marked black and white head, and a yellow crest. It is very noisy, is common in the neighborhood of houses, and builds a big domed nest. It is really a big, heavy kingbird, fiercer and more powerful than any northern kingbird. I saw them assail not only the big but the small hawks with fearlessness, driving them in headlong flight. They not only capture insects, but pounce on mice, small frogs, lizards, and little snakes, rob birds’ nests of the fledgling young, and catch tadpoles and even small fish.
Two of the tyrants which I observed are like two with which I grew fairly familiar in Texas. The scissor-tail is common throughout the open country, and the long tail feathers, which seem at times to hamper its flight, attract attention whether the bird is in flight or perched on a tree. It has a habit of occasionally soaring into the air and descending in loops and spirals. The scarlet tyrant I saw in the orchards and gardens. The male is a fascinating little bird, coal-black above, while his crested head and the body beneath are brilliant scarlet.

===Chinese===

它们自会来找你；一经发现，便冲着天地大喊大叫，唯恐天下不知。在下巴拉那河的沼泽里，我看见成群的绯红头椋鸟栖在芦苇梢上；雌鸟羽色之醒目不逊雄鸟，乌黑的身子配着鲜红的头，在它们生息的环境里想不惹眼都不可能。在西边的平原上，我看见成群美丽的玫胸椋鸟；红头椋鸟仿佛存心招摇，这些椋鸟却伏在地上，把红胸藏起，只想躲开人们的注意。湿地里还有黄肩黑椋鸟，牛鹂数量极多。
但我见过的最惹眼的鸟，当属霸鹟科的各种——我们北美的王霸鹟便是这科里最广为人知的例子。这一科在阿根廷无论种类还是数量都极多。有些种类在羽色和习性上——有一两种连形体上——都如此出众，连最不留心的人也难以忽视。我所见的这类鸟中，最不显眼却依然相当显眼的是“必恩特维多”（bientevido）——上体褐色，下体黄色，头上的黑白纹样分明，还有一顶黄色羽冠。它聒噪得很，宅院附近处处常见，筑一个大大的圆顶巢。它其实就是一种大而壮硕的王霸鹟，比北方的任何王霸鹟都更凶猛有力。我见它们无所畏惧地攻击大小鹰隼，追得那些仓皇飞逃。它们不仅捕虫，还扑食老鼠、小蛙、蜥蜴和小蛇，劫掠鸟巢里的雏鸟，捉蝌蚪，甚至捉小鱼。
我观察到的霸鹟中，有两种同我在得克萨斯颇为熟悉的两种相似。剪尾霸鹟在开阔乡野处处可见，那对长尾羽有时似乎碍事碍飞行，但无论它凌空飞舞还是栖在树上，都引人注目。它有个习惯，时不时腾空而起，再打着圈、绕着螺旋降下来。绯红霸鹟我在果园和花园里见过。雄鸟是只迷人的小鸟，上体煤黑，戴冠的头和下体却是耀眼的绯红。

===Original===

He utters his rapid, low-voiced musical trill in the air, rising with fluttering wings to a height of a hundred feet, hovering while he sings, and then falling back to earth. The color of the bird and the character of his performance attract the attention of every observer, bird, beast, or man, within reach of vision.
The red-backed tyrant is utterly unlike any of his kind in the United States, and until I looked him up in Sclater and Hudson’s ornithology I never dreamed that he belonged to this family. He﻿—for only the male is so brightly colored﻿—is coal-black with a dull-red back. I saw these birds on December 1 near Barilloche, out on the bare Patagonian plains. They behaved like pipits or longspurs, running actively over the ground in the same manner and showing the same restlessness and the same kind of flight. But whereas pipits are inconspicuous, the red-backs at once attracted attention by the contrast between their bold coloring and the grayish or yellowish tones of the ground along which they ran. The silver-bill tyrant, however, is much more conspicuous; I saw it in the same neighborhood as the red-back and also in many other places. The male is jet-black, with white bill and wings. He runs about on the ground like a pipit, but also frequently perches on some bush to go through a strange flight-song performance. He perches motionless, bolt upright, and even then his black coloring advertises him for a quarter of a mile round about. But every few minutes he springs up into the air to the height of twenty or thirty feet, the white wings flashing in contrast to the black body, screams and gyrates, and then instantly returns to his former post and resumes his erect pose of waiting. It is hard to imagine a more conspicuous bird than the silver-bill; but the next and last tyrant flycatcher of which I shall speak possesses on the whole the most advertising coloration of any small bird I have ever seen in the open country, and moreover this advertising coloration exists in both sexes and throughout the year. It is a brilliant white, all over, except the long wing-quills and the ends of the tail-feathers, which are black. The first one I saw, at a very long distance, I thought must be an albino. It perches on the top of a bush or tree watching for its prey, and it shines in the sun like a silver mirror.

===Chinese===

它在空中发出急促而低柔的乐音颤鸣：振翅上升到百英尺高处，悬停歌唱，然后再落回地面。它的羽色加上这番做派，凡是目力所及的观察者——无论鸟、兽还是人——无不为之侧目。
红背霸鹟同美国的任何同类都截然不同，直到在斯克莱特与哈德逊的鸟类学著作里查到它，我才晓得它也属于这一科。它——只有雄鸟羽色如此鲜亮——通体煤黑，背部暗红。十二月一日，我在巴里洛切附近空旷的巴塔哥尼亚平原上见到了它们。它们的做派像鹨或铁爪鹀，同样在地面活泼奔走，同样躁动不安，飞行也如出一辙。但鹨毫不起眼，红背霸鹟却一眼惹目：它们鲜明的羽色同脚下灰黄的地色对比太强烈了。至于银嘴霸鹟，那就更扎眼了；在红背霸鹟出没的同一带，还有许多别的地方，我都见过它。雄鸟乌黑，嘴和翅膀却是白的。它像鹨一样在地上跑动，却也常常栖上灌木，来一段奇特的“飞行演唱”表演。它一动不动、笔直地立在那里，即便如此，那一身黑色也令方圆四分之一英里内都知道它在哪儿。但每隔几分钟，它便腾空跃起二三十英尺高，双翅雪白与黑身相映生辉，尖叫着回旋飞舞，随即又立回原处，恢复笔直的守望姿态。很难想象还有比银嘴霸鹟更显眼的鸟了；但下面要说的最后一种霸鹟，总体而言是我平生在旷野所见羽色最“广而告之”的小鸟，而且这种招摇的羽色雌雄皆备、四时不变。它通体皎白，只有长长的翼羽和尾羽末端是黑的。我第一次远远望见它时，还以为是一只白化个体。它栖在灌木或树梢上守候猎物，在太阳底下闪闪发亮，如同一面银镜。

===Original===

Every hawk, cat, or man must see it; no one can help seeing it.
These common Argentine birds, most of them of the open country, and all of them with a strikingly advertising coloration, are interesting because of their beauty and their habits. They are also interesting because they offer such illuminating examples of the truth that many of the most common and successful birds not merely lack a concealing coloration, but possess a coloration which is in the highest degree revealing. The coloration and the habits of most of these birds are such that every hawk or other foe that can see at all must have its attention attracted to them. Evidently in their cases neither the coloration nor any habit of concealment based on the coloration is a survival factor, and this although they live in a land teeming with bird-eating hawks. Among the higher vertebrates there are many known factors which have influence, some in one set of cases, some in another set of cases, in the development and preservation of species.
