有些小小的私人便利之物，有时会变成必需。就我自己而言，其一便是一支小手电筒——原为观测星辰时读经纬仪（theodolite）或六分仪的游标而带。它每夜都用，用途繁多。虫多的地方，实际情况是：晚饭一毕，人就得躲进吊床蚊帐的庇护。正是在这种时候，我发现这支电筒帮了大忙。半躺在吊床里，我把电筒杆夹在左腋，日记摊在膝上，把当天的记录补齐。有时就着它柔和稳定的光读读书。令我吃惊的是，一节电池竟用了近一个月。

===Original===

When forced to pick out a camping spot after dark, an experience which comes to every traveller in the tropics in the rainy season, we found its light very helpful. Neither rain nor wind could put it out and the light could be directed wherever needed. The charges should be calculated on the plan of one for every three weeks. The acetylene lamp for camp illumination is an advance over the kerosene lantern. It has been found that for equal weight the carbide will give more light than kerosene or candle. The carbide should be put in small containers, for each time a box is opened some of the contents turns into gas from contact with the moist air.
**Tools.**﻿—Three or four good axes, several billhooks, a good hatchet with hammer head and nail-puller should be in the tool kit. In addition, each man should be provided with a belt knife and a machete with sheath. Collins makes the best machetes. His axes, too, are excellent. The billhook, called *foice* in Brazil, is a most valuable tool for clearing away small trees, vines, and under-growths. It is marvellous how quickly an experienced hand can clear the ground in a forest with one of these instruments. All of these tools should have handles of second-growth American hickory of first quality; and several extra handles should be taken along. The list of tools should be completed with a small outfit of pliers, tweezers, files, etc.﻿—the character, of course, depending upon the mechanical ability of the traveller and the scientific instruments he has with him that might need repairs.
**Survey Instruments.**﻿—The choice of instruments will depend largely upon the character of the work intended. If a compass survey will suffice, there is nothing better than the cavalry sketching board used in the United States Army for reconnaissance. With a careful hand it approaches the high degree of perfection attained by the plane-table method. It is particularly adapted for river survey and, after one gets accustomed to its use, it is very simple. If the prismatic compass is preferred, nothing smaller than two and one half inches in diameter should be used. In the smaller sizes the magnet is not powerful enough to move the dial quickly or accurately.
Several good pocket compasses must be provided. They should all have good-sized needles with the north end well marked and degrees engraved in metal.

===Chinese===

雨季热带旅行者人人都免不了摸黑择地扎营的经历——这种时候我们发现它的光极有用。风雨都吹不灭它，光可指向任何需要之处。电池应按每3周一充的计划备足。营地照明用乙炔灯，比煤油灯先进。已经证实，等重之下，电石发出的光多于煤油或蜡烛。电石应装小容器——因为每次开盒，总有一部分遇潮气化作气体跑掉。
**工具。**——工具包里应有三四把好斧、几把钩刀、一把带锤头和起钉器的手斧。此外每人应配一把腰刀和一把带鞘砍刀。柯林斯的砍刀最好，斧头也极佳。钩刀在巴西叫*福伊塞*（foice），是清小树、藤蔓和下层灌丛的利器。熟手操之在林中开路清场，其快无比。这些工具的柄都应用头等美国再生山核桃木；并多带几根备用。工具单再补一小套钳子、镊子、锉刀等——具体如何，当然视旅行者的机械手艺和他所携可能需要修理的科学仪器而定。
**测量仪器。**——仪器之选，大半取决于工作的性质。若罗盘测量即可，没有什么比得上美军侦察用的骑兵测图板。细心之人用它，可以接近平板仪法所达到的高精度。它尤其适合河道测量，用惯之后非常简单。若偏好棱镜罗盘，直径不得小于2.5英寸。更小的型号磁力不足，转动刻度盘既迟又不准。必须多备几只好袖珍罗盘。都应有大号磁针，北端标识分明，刻度刻在金属上。

===Original===

If the floating dial is preferred it should be of aluminum and nothing smaller than two and one half inches, for the same reason as mentioned above regarding the prismatic compass.
Expense should not be spared if it is necessary to secure good compasses. Avoid paper dials and leather cases which absorb moisture. The compass case should allow taking apart for cleaning and drying.
The regular chronometer movement, because of its delicacy, is out of the question for rough land or water travel. We had with us a small-sized half-chronometer movement recently brought out by the Waltham Company as a yacht chronometer. It gave a surprisingly even rate under the most adverse conditions. I was sorry to lose it in the rapids of the Papagaio when our canoes went down.
The watches should be waterproof with strong cases, and several should be taken. It would be well to have a dozen cheap but good watches and the same number of compasses for use around camp and for gifts or trade along the line of travel. Money is of no value after one leaves the settlements. I was surprised to find that many of the rubber hunters were not provided with compasses, and I listened to an American who told of having been lost in the depths of the great forest where for days he lived on monkey meat secured with his rifle until he found his way to the river. He had no compass and could not get one. I was sorry I had none to give; I had lost mine in the rapids.
For the determination of latitude and longitude there is nothing better than a small four or five inch theodolite not over fifteen pounds in weight. It should have a good prism eyepiece with an angle tube attached so it would not be necessary to break one’s neck in reading high altitudes. For days we travelled in the direction the sun was going, with altitudes varying from 88° to 90°. Because of these high altitudes of the sun the sextant with artificial horizon could not be used unless one depended upon star observations altogether, an uncertain dependence because of the many cloudy nights.
**Barometers.**﻿—The Goldsmith form of direct-reading aneroid is the most accurate portable instrument and, of course, should be compared with a standard mercurial at the last weather-bureau station.
**Thermometers.

===Chinese===

若偏好浮动刻度盘，则应用铝制，且不小于2.5英寸——理由同上述棱镜罗盘。若需置办好罗盘，费用不必省。忌纸面刻度盘和吸潮的皮盒。罗盘盒应可拆开清洗晾干。
制式天文钟机芯过于娇贵，粗放的陆路水路旅行想都不用想。我们带着一支沃尔瑟姆公司新推出的小型半天文钟机芯，作游艇天文钟之用。它在最恶劣的条件下走时均匀得出奇。我们的独木舟在帕帕加约河险滩翻沉时，我痛失了它。
手表应防水、表壳坚固，且应多带。最好备一打价廉物美的表和同样数目的罗盘，营中自用，沿途送礼或交易。一离开聚落，钱便毫无用处。我惊讶地发现，许多割胶人竟没有罗盘。我听一位美国人讲过：他在大森林深处迷了路，一连数日靠步枪猎猴肉活命，直到找路回到河边。他没有罗盘，也搞不到。我遗憾自己无罗盘可赠——我的也在险滩里丢了。
测定经纬度，最好的莫过于一台4或5英寸的小型经纬仪，重不逾15磅。应配带折角镜筒的良好棱镜目镜，免得读高角时扭断脖子。我们曾一连几天朝着太阳走的方向行进，太阳高度在88°到90°之间。由于太阳高度如此之大，六分仪配人工水平面便无法使用，除非全凭观星——而多云之夜太多，这条靠不住。
**气压计。**——戈德史密斯式直读空盒气压计（aneroid）是最精确的便携仪器，当然，应在最后一个气象台站与标准水银气压计核对。
**温度计。**

===Original===

**﻿—A swing thermometer, with wet and dry bulbs for determination of the amount of moisture in the air, and the maximum and minimum thermometer of the signal-service or weather-bureau type should be provided, with a case to protect them from injury.
A tape measure with metric scale of measurements on one side and feet and inches on the other is most important. Two small, light waterproof cases could be constructed and packed with scientific instruments, data, and spare clothing and yet not exceed the weight limit of flotation. In transit by pack-train these two cases would form but one mule load.
**Photographic.**﻿—From the experience gained in several fields of exploration it seems to me that the voyager should limit himself to one small-sized camera, which he can always have with him, and then carry a duplicate of it, soldered in tin, in the baggage. The duplicate need not be equipped with as expensive a lens and shutter as the camera carried for work; 3¼ × 4¼ is a good size. Nothing larger than 3¼ × 5½ is advised. We carried the 3A special Kodak and found it a light, strong, and effective instrument. It seems to me that the ideal form of instrument would be one with a front board large enough to contain an adapter fitted for three lenses. For the 3¼ × 4¼:
The camera should be made of metal and fitted with focal-plane shutter and direct viewfinder.
A sole leather case with shoulder-strap should contain the camera and lenses, with an extra roll of films, all within instant reach, so that a lens could be changed without any loss of time.
Plates, of course, are the best, but their weight and frailty, with difficulty of handling, rule them out of the question. The roll film is the best, as the film pack sticks together and the stubs pull off in the moist, hot climate. The films should be purchased in rolls of six exposures, each roll in a tin, the cover sealed with surgical tape. Twelve of these tubes should be soldered in a tin box. In places where the air is charged with moisture a roll of films should not be left in a camera over twenty-four hours.
Tank development is best for the field. The tanks provided for developing by the Kodak Company are best for fixing also. A nest of tanks would be a convenience; one tank should be kept separate for the fixing-bath.

===Chinese===

**——应备一支干湿球摆动温度计，用以测空气中水分含量；再加气象部门式最高最低温度计；并配护盒防损。
一面公制、一面英尺英寸的卷尺极重要。可制两只轻质防水小箱，分装科学仪器、资料和换洗衣物，仍不超漂浮限重。驮运时，两箱合并正好一骡之驮。
**摄影。**——凭几处探险所得的经验，我看旅行者应限用一架小型相机，随身携带；再在行李里带一架同型备机，锡封防潮。备机的镜头和快门不必像工作机那么昂贵；3¼×4¼是合适的尺寸。不建议大于3¼×5½。我们带的是柯达3A特型机，发现它轻巧、坚固而有效。依我看，理想的机型，前板要大得装下可配三只镜头的转接盘。就3¼×4¼而言：
相机应为金属制，配帘幕快门和直视取景器。
一只带肩带的牛皮机箱，装相机、镜头外加一卷备用胶卷，处处唾手可得，换镜头毫不耗时。
玻璃干板当然最好，但重量、易碎和搬运之难使它出局。卷装胶片最佳——散页软片包在湿热气候里会粘作一团，小耳片一扯就掉。胶卷应购6张一卷的，每卷装一只铁筒，筒盖以医用胶布密封。12支这样的筒再锡焊入一只铁皮箱。空气饱含水分的地方，一卷胶片在相机里放置不得超过24小时。
罐显最适合野外。柯达公司的显影罐作定影罐也最佳。一套大小套罐很便利；应单留一罐专用于定影液。

===Original===

As suggested in the Kodak circular, for tropical development a large-size tank can be used for holding the freezing mixture of hypo. This same tank would become the fixing tank after development. In the rainy season it is a difficult matter to dry films. Development in the field, with washing water at 80° F., is a patience-trying operation. It has occurred to me that a small air-pump with a supply of chloride of calcium in small tubes might solve the problem of preserving films in the tropics. The air-pump and supply of chloride of calcium would not be as heavy or bulky as the tanks and powders needed for development. By means of the air-pump the films could be sealed in tin tubes free from moisture and kept thus until arrival at home or at a city where the air was fairly dry and cold water for washing could be had.
---
While I cordially agree with most of the views expressed by Mr. Fiala, there are some as to which I disagree; for instance, we came very strongly to the conclusion, in descending the Dúvida, where bulk was of great consequence, that the films should be in rolls of ten or twelve exposures. I doubt whether the four-barrel gun would be practical; but this is a matter of personal taste.

===Chinese===

如柯达说明册所建议，热带显影可用一只大罐盛放海波冰混合液。显影之后，此罐便转作定影罐。雨季晾干胶片是件难事。野外显影，洗片水温高达华氏80度，是磨人的活计。我想到一个办法：小型气泵加若干小管氯化钙，或许能解决热带保存胶片的难题。气泵和氯化钙比显影所需的罐罐粉粉轻得多小得多。用气泵把胶片封入无潮锡筒，如此保存，直到回到家中或抵达一座空气相当干燥、又有冷水洗片的城市。
---
菲亚拉先生的高见我衷心赞同者居多，但也有不苟同之处；例如，在体积举足轻重的杜维达河下行中，我们得出过非常明确的结论：胶片应以10或12张一卷为宜。四管枪是否实用，我存疑；不过这纯属个人口味。

## Appendix C My Letter of May 1 to General Lauro Müller / 附录C 我5月1日致劳罗·米勒将军的信
===Original===

The first report on the expedition, made by me immediately after my arrival at Manaos, and published in Rio de Janeiro upon its receipt, is as follows:

===Chinese===

我抵达马瑙斯后立即写就的关于考察队的首份报告，到件后即在里约热内卢发表，全文如下：

===Original===

> I wish first to express my profound acknowledgments to you personally and to the other members of the Brazilian Government whose generous courtesy alone rendered possible the Expedição Scientifica Roosevelt-Rondon. I wish also to express my high admiration and regard for Colonel Rondon and his associates who have been my colleagues in this work of exploration. In the third place I wish to point out that what we have just done was rendered possible only by the hard and perilous labor of the Brazilian Telegraphic Commission in the unexplored western wilderness of Matto Grosso during the last seven years. We have had a hard and somewhat dangerous but very successful trip. No less than six weeks were spent in slowly and with peril and exhausting labor forcing our way down through what seemed a literally endless succession of rapids and cataracts. For forty-eight days we saw no human being. In passing these rapids we lost five of the seven canoes with which we started and had to build others. One of our best men lost his life in the rapids. Under the strain one of the men went completely bad, shirked all his work, stole his comrades’ food and when punished by the sergeant he with cold-blooded deliberation murdered the sergeant and fled into the wilderness. Colonel Rondon’s dog running ahead of him while hunting, was shot by two Indians; by his death he in all probability saved the life of his master. We have put on the map a river about 1,500 kilometres in length running from just south of the 13th degree to north of the 5th degree and the biggest affluent of the Madeira. Until now its upper course has been utterly unknown to everyone, and its lower course although known for years to the rubbermen utterly unknown to all cartographers. Its source is between the 12th and 13th parallels of latitude south, and between longitude 59° and longitude 60° west from Greenwich. We embarked on it about at latitude 12° 1′ south and longitude 60° 18 west. After that its entire course was between the 60th and 61st degrees of longitude approaching the latter most closely about in latitude 8° 15′. The first rapids were at Navaïté in 11° 44′ and after that they were continuous and very difficult and dangerous until the rapids named after the murdered sergeant Paishon in 11° 12′. At 11° 23′ the river received the Rio Kermit from the left.

===Chinese===

> 首先，我谨向您本人及巴西政府其他成员致以深切的谢忱——唯有诸位慷慨的礼遇，罗斯福-龙东科学考察队（Expedição Scientifica Roosevelt-Rondon）方得以成行。我还要向隆东上校及其同仁表达崇高的敬佩与问候——他们是我此次勘察工作中并肩的同事。第三，我要指出：我们刚刚完成的事业，唯有仰仗巴西电报委员会过去七年间在马托格罗索西部未勘荒野中的艰苦而凶险的劳动，方才成为可能。
> 我们的旅程艰辛而略带凶险，但非常成功。我们足足花了六个星期，冒着危险、耗尽力气，缓缓闯过一道接一道仿佛无穷无尽的险滩瀑布。整整四十八天，我们没有见到一个人影。过这些险滩时，出发时的七条独木舟折损了五条，只得另造新舟。我们一名最好的伙计在险滩中丧了命。在重压之下，有一个人彻底堕落：逃避一切劳作，偷窃同伴的食物；中士惩罚他时，他竟蓄意冷血地杀害了中士，逃入荒野。隆东上校打猎时跑在前面的狗，被两个印第安人射杀；它一死，多半救了主人的命。
> 我们已在地图上标出一条约1500公里长的河：起自南纬十三度以南不远处，止于南纬五度以北，是马代拉河最大的支流。迄今为止，其上游对所有人全然未知；下游虽为采胶人熟知多年，却对一切制图者全然未知。其源头在南纬12度与13度之间、格林尼治西经59度与60度之间。我们登船处约在南纬12°1′、西经60°18′。此后全程河道都在西经60度与61度之间，以南纬8°15′处最逼近后者。第一处险滩在纳瓦伊泰，南纬11°44′；此后险滩连绵不断，艰难凶险，直到以遇害的派雄中士命名的派雄滩，南纬11°12′。南纬11°23′处，克米特河自左汇入。

===Original===

At 11° 22′ the Marciano Avila entered it from the right. At 11° 18′ the Taunay entered from the left. At 10° 58′ the Cardozo entered from the right. At 10° 24′ we encountered the first rubber man. The Rio Branco entered from the left at 9° 38′. We camped at 8° 49′ or approximately the boundary line between Matto Grosso and Amazonas. The confluence with the upper Aripuanan, which entered from the right, was in 7° 34′. The mouth where it entered the Madeira was in about 5° 30′. The stream we have followed down is that which rises farthest away from the mouth and its general course is almost due north.
> My dear Sir, I thank you from my heart for the chance to take part in this great work of exploration.
> With high regard and respect, believe me
> Very sincerely yours,
> Theodore Roosevelt.

===Chinese===

> 南纬11°22′处，马尔恰诺·阿维拉河自右汇入。南纬11°18′处，陶奈河自左汇入。南纬10°58′处，卡多佐河自右汇入。南纬10°24′处，我们遇到第一个采胶人。布兰科河于南纬9°38′自左汇入。我们在南纬8°49′处扎营，约当马托格罗索州与亚马逊州的州界。与自右来汇的阿里普阿南河上游的合流处，在南纬7°34′。它注入马代拉河的河口，约在南纬5°30′。我们循流而下的这一支，正是离河口最远的源头；其大流向几乎正北。
> 亲爱的先生，我从心底感谢您给我参与这项伟大勘察事业的机会。
> 谨致崇高的敬意与问候，请相信我是
> 您最诚挚的，
> 西奥多·罗斯福。

## Endnotes / 尾注
===Original===

1. Pronounced “sairtown,” as nearly as, with our preposterous methods of spelling and pronunciation, I can render it. ↩︎
2. The first four days, before we struck the upper rapids, and during which we made nearly seventy kilometres, are of course not included when I speak of our making our way down the rapids. ↩︎
3. The above account of all the circumstances connected with the murder was read to and approved as correct by all six members of the expedition. ↩︎
4. I hope that this year the Ananás, or Pineapple, will also be put on the map. One of Colonel Rondon’s subordinates is to attempt the descent of the river. We passed the headwaters of the Pineapple on the high plateau, very possibly we passed its mouth, although it is also possible that it empties into the Canama or Tapajos. But it will not be “put on the map” until someone descends and finds out where, as a matter of fact, it really does go. ↩︎
5. It would be well if a geographical society of standing would investigate the formal and official charges made by Colonel Rondon, an officer and gentleman of the highest repute, against Mr. Savage Landor. Colonel Rondon, in an official report to the Brazilian Government, has written a scathing review of Mr. Landor. He states that Mr. Savage Landor did not perform, and did not even attempt to perform, the work he had contracted to do in exploration for the Brazilian Government. Mr. Landor had asserted and promised that he would go through unknown country along the line of eleven degrees latitude south, and, as Colonel Rondon states, it was because of this proposal of his that the Brazilian Government gave him material financial assistance in advance. However, Colonel Rondon sets forth that Mr. Landor did not keep his word or make any serious effort to fulfil his moral obligation to do as he had said he would do. In a letter to me under date of May 1, 1914﻿—a letter which has been published in full in France﻿—Colonel Rondon goes at length into the question of what territory Mr. Landor had traversed. Colonel Rondon states that﻿—excepting on one occasion, when Mr. Landor, wandering off a beaten trail, immediately got lost and shortly returned to his starting-point without making any discoveries﻿—he kept to old, well-travelled routes. One sentence of the colonel’s letter to me runs as follows: “I can guarantee to you that in Brazil Mr.

===Chinese===

1. 发音近于“塞尔唐”——凭我们那些荒唐的拼写和发音法，我只能凑合到这个地步了。 ↩︎
2. 头四天——尚未抵达上游险滩之前——我们走了近七十公里；这四天当然不算在我所说的“闯滩下行”之内。 ↩︎
3. 上述与凶案有关的全部情节，已向考察队六名成员宣读，并经众人核准无误。 ↩︎
4. 我希望今年阿纳南斯河（意即“菠萝河”）也能上图。隆东上校的一名部属将尝试顺流而下。我们在高原上经过了菠萝河的源头，也很可能经过了它的河口——虽然它也可能注入卡纳马河或塔帕若斯河。但在有人顺流而下、查明它究竟流向何方之前，它还上不了图。 ↩︎
5. 若有一个有地位的地—理学会，调查一下隆东上校——一位声誉至隆的军官和绅士——对萨维奇·兰多先生提出的正式官方指控，那将大有裨益。隆东上校在呈巴西政府的官方报告中，对兰多先生写了一篇痛切的评述。他指出，萨维奇·兰多先生没有履行、甚至没有尝试履行他为巴西政府承担的勘察工作。兰多先生曾宣称并承诺沿南纬十一度线穿越未知地域；据隆东上校说，正是这项提议，使巴西政府预付给他可观的财政资助。然而隆东上校申明，兰多先生并未守信，也没有作出任何认真的努力，去履行他照其所言行事的道义责任。在1914年5月1日写给我的一封信中——该信已在法国全文发表——隆东上校详尽探讨了兰多先生究竟走过哪些地域的问题。隆东上校指出：除了一次——那次兰多先生离开熟路乱走，随即迷路，不久便折返出发点，一无所获——他走的全是走熟了的老路。上校信中有一句话是这样说的：“我可以向你担保，在巴西，兰多先生

===Original===

Landor did not cross a hand’s breadth of land that had not been explored, the greater part of it many centuries ago.” As regards Mr. Landor’s sole and brief experience in leaving a beaten route, Colonel Rondon states that at São Manoel Mr. Landor engaged from Senhor José Sotero Barreto (the revenue officer of Matto Grosso, at São Manoel) a guide to lead him across a well-travelled trail which connects the Tapajos with the Madeira via the Canama. The guide, however, got lost, and after a few days they all returned to the point of departure instead of going through to the Canama.
 Senhor Barreto, a gentleman of high standing, related this last incident to Fiala when Fiala descended the Tapajos (and, by the way, Fiala’s trip down the Papagaio, Juruena, and Tapajos was infinitely more important than all the work Mr. Landor did in South America put together). Lieutenants Pyrineus and Mello, mentioned in the body of this work, informed me that they accompanied Mr. Landor on most of his overland trip before he embarked on the Arinos, and that he simply followed the highroad or else the telegraph-line, and furthermore, Colonel Rondon states that the Indians whom Mr. Landor encountered and photographed were those educated at the missions.
 Colonel Rondon’s official report to the Brazilian Government and his letter to me are of interest to all geographers and other scientific men who have any concern with the alleged discoveries of Mr. Landor. They contain very grave charges, with which it is not necessary for me to deal. Suffice it to say that Mr. Landor’s accounts of his alleged exploration cannot be considered as entitled to the slightest serious consideration until he has satisfactorily and in detail answered Colonel Rondon; and this he has thus far signally failed to do.
 Fortunately, there are numerous examples of exactly the opposite type of work. From the days of Humboldt and Spix and Martius to the present time, German explorers have borne a conspicuous part in the exploration of South America. As representatives of the men and women who have done such capital work, who have fronted every hazard and hardship and labored in the scientific spirit, and who have added greatly to our fund of geographic, biologic, and ethnographic knowledge, I may mention Miss Snethlage and Herr Karl von den Steinen. ↩︎

===Chinese===

没有跨过一掌宽的未经勘察之地——那些地方大半早在许多世纪前就勘察过了。”
至于兰多先生唯一一次短暂离开熟路的经历，隆东上校说：在圣马努埃尔，兰多先生向若泽·索特罗·巴雷托先生（马托格罗索驻圣马努埃尔的税务官）雇了一名向导，领他走一条连接塔帕若斯河与马代拉河、取道卡纳马河的老路。然而向导迷了路；几天后，一行人全数折返出发点，没有走到卡纳马河。
巴雷托先生是位地位崇高的绅士；菲亚拉下塔帕若斯河时，他把这段始末讲给了菲亚拉（顺带一提，菲亚拉沿帕帕加约河、茹鲁埃纳河和塔帕若斯河而下的那次行程，比兰多先生在南美洲的全部工作加在一起还要重要无数倍）。本书正文提到过的皮里纽斯中尉和梅洛中尉告诉我：兰多先生登阿里诺斯河之前的大部分陆路行程，他们都随行；他不过循着大路或电报线走；此外，隆东上校还说，兰多先生遇到并拍摄的印第安人，都是传教团教化过的。
隆东上校呈巴西政府的官方报告和他写给我的信，对一切关注兰多先生所谓发现的地理学家和其他科学人士都有价值。它们包含非常严重的指控，无须我来处置。只消说一句：在兰多先生就隆东上校的指控作出令人满意的、详尽的答复之前，他那套所谓勘察的记述，不配得到丝毫认真的考虑；而到目前为止，他在这方面败得一塌糊涂。
所幸，截然相反类型的工作例证很多。从洪堡以及施皮克斯与马蒂乌斯的时代至今，德国探察者一直在南美洲的勘察中扮演显要角色。作为这一类男女的表率——他们作出了第一流的工作，直面一切艰险，以科学精神劳作，为我们的地理学、生物学与民族学知识宝库作出了巨大增益——我可以举出斯内特拉格小姐和卡尔·冯·登·施泰因先生。 ↩︎

Colonel Roosevelt and Colonel Rondon with bush deer. We hung the buck in a tree. From a photograph by Fiala.

88. Sketch map of the unknown river christened Rio Roosevelt, and subsequently Rio Téodoro, by direction of the Brazilian Government. This map was prepared by Colonel Roosevelt from his journal and the diaries of Cherrie and of Kermit Roosevelt, the war having prevented the arrival of the map prepared by Lieutenant Lyra. The Ananás may be the headwaters of the Cardozo or of the Aripuanan, or it may flow into the Canumá or Tapajos; it will not be put on the map until it is actually descended.

88. 那条被命名为罗斯福河——后奉巴西政府之命又改称特奥多罗河（Rio Téodoro）——的未知河流的草图。此图由罗斯福上校依据本人日志及切里、克米特·罗斯福的日记绘制；因欧战爆发，利拉中尉所测之图未能送到。阿纳南斯河可能是卡多佐河或阿里普阿南河的源头，也可能流入卡努马河或塔帕若斯河；在有人实地顺流而下之前，它不会上图。
